Pro-étale motives and solid rigidity
Raphaël Ruimy, Swann Tubach, Sebastian Wolf

Abstract
We introduce coefficient systems of pro-étale motives and pro-étale motivic spectra with coefficients in any condensed ring spectrum and show that they afford the six operations. Over locally étale bounded schemes, étale motivic spectra embed into pro-étale motivic spectra. We then use the framework of condensed category theory to define a solidification process for any ℤ ^ \widehat{\mathbb{Z}} -linear condensed category. Pro-étale motives naturally enhance to a condensed category and we show that their solidification is very close to the category of solid sheaves defined by Fargues-Scholze, suitably modified to work on schemes: this is a rigidity result. As a consequence, we obtain that in contrast with the rigid-analytic setting, solid sheaves on schemes afford the six operations, and we obtain a solid realization functor of motives, extending the ℓ \ell -adic realization functor. The solid realization functor is compatible with change of coefficients, which allows one to recover the ℚ ℓ \mathbb{Q}_{\ell} -adic realization functor while remaining in a setting of presentable categories.



Introduction

Coefficient systems for
\ell
-adic cohomology

Betti cohomology can be expressed in terms of sheaves on the topological space of complex points; likewise de Rham cohomology is best understood through the theory of
\mathscr{D}
-modules; finally torsion étale cohomology is defined through torsion sheaves on the small étale site. In all of the above examples, cohomology is expressed by means of a
coefficient system
: in Grothendieck’s vision, this is a category fibered over the category of schemes, equipped with additional functoriality (such as the six operations) allowing for the definition of cohomology with coefficients in such systems.

Finding well suited coefficient systems for
\ell
-adic cohomology is more challenging. Let us first remark that there should actually be two such coefficient systems: the
big
(or more accurately
presentable
) one and the
small
one that should lie inside of it as the subcategory of constructible objects. The theory of constructible
\ell
-adic sheaves is well understood. Its initial construction was done for finite-type schemes over a field in
[
BBD82
]
by taking a (homotopy) limit:

\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}(-,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})=\lim_{n}\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}(-,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}\mathbb{Z})

with the right-hand side given by constructible sheaves on the small étale site. Ekedahl then constructed a presentable coefficient system over noetherian schemes in
[
Eke90
]
. The latter can actually be recovered by taking the
\ell
-completion
\mathrm{D}((-)_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z})^{\wedge}_{\ell}
of the derived category of the abelian category of étale sheaves (this is the approach of
[
CD16
]
).
One downside of Ekedahl’s coefficient system is that it does not interact well with rationalization, in fact

\mathrm{D}((-)_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z})^{\wedge}_{\ell}\otimes\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Q})\simeq 0.

In order to obtain a well behaved theory one needs to keep track of the natural topologies on the rings
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
.
The now classical idea from
[
BS15
]
is that instead of
\ell
-completing in the categorical sense as in Ekedahl’s theory, one can keep track of these topologies by using the
pro-étale site
of a scheme.
Indeed, one can see both
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
as sheaves on the pro-étale site in a manner that takes into account their topology.
A natural candidate for a coefficient system is now the derived category
\mathrm{D}((-)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
of the abelian category of
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
-modules in the pro-étale site.
Indeed, in this case one has

\mathrm{D}((-)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\otimes\mathrm{D}(\mathbb{Q})\simeq\mathrm{D}((-)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}),

as expected.
This system is however far from having all the desired functoriality properties.
For instance, the proper base change theorem already fails for this coefficient system
[
HHL+25
, Remark 7.15]
.
One of the goals of this work is to provide a theory that has all the expected functoriality properties and is still compatible with rationalization in the above sense.

Voevodsky motives and their
\ell
-adic realization

Another motivation comes from the theory of Voevodsky motives and especially
\ell
-adic realization functors.
Voevodsky étale motives form the universal étale-local coefficient system for schemes
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
; the latter (in particular all the key functorialities) was constructed in
[
Voe92
,
Ayo14
,
CD16
]
. The theory of
\ell
-adic cohomology is connected to the theory of étale motives via
\ell
-adic realization functors.
A powerful tool in understanding these realization functors is the
rigidity theorem
(see
e.g.

[
BH21
]
for the most general such theorems): left Kan-extension from étale to smooth
X
-schemes gives a natural map

\mathrm{D}((-)_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}

which becomes an equivalence after
\ell
-completion.
Thus
\ell
-completion provides a natural map to Ekedahl’s coefficient system

\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to(\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})^{\wedge}_{\ell}\simeq\mathrm{D}((-)_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z})^{\wedge}_{\ell},

which we call the
\ell
-adic realization
.
Another way of thinking about the incompatibility of
\mathrm{D}((-)_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z})^{\wedge}_{\ell}
with rationalization mentioned above, is the fact
\mathbb{Q}
is sent to
0
by this functor.
Furthermore, one can deduce from the above rigidity theorem that
\ell
-adic cohomology is representable in
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
by the
\ell
-completion
\mathbb{Z}^{\wedge}_{\ell}
of the unit. This motive has the unpleasant feature that it is not stable under base change in mixed characteristic (see
[
Cis25
]
): it is shown in loc. cit. that such stability would imply
independence-of-
\ell
results, which are actually too strong to be true.

The other main objective of this paper is to build a new framework in which
\ell
-adic realization functors are exceptionally well-behaved: the idea is to construct a coefficient system
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
of
pro-étale motives
, which is an enlargement of
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
and allows one to consider motives with coefficients in topological rings.
One then again has a canonical map

\mathrm{D}((-)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\to\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(-,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})

which we would very much like to be an equivalence:
\ell
-adic realization functors would then simply be given by extending scalars to the topological ring
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
which would thus represent
\ell
-adic cohomology and be stable under base change.
This is however far too optimistic as the right-hand side is a coefficient system while the left-hand side is not as it does not have proper base change.
To salvage this situation, we introduce a way of
solidifying
the above categories, building on ideas of
[
CS21
]
and
[
FS24
]
.

Solid sheaves on schemes

Condensed abelian groups (sheaves of abelian groups on the pro-étale site of a point) can be thought of as a model for topological abelian groups.
Solidity then aims to describe a version of non-archimedean completeness: solid abelian groups form a full subcategory of condensed abelian groups and for instance contain
p
-adic Banach spaces as a full subcategory. In the rigid-analytic world, Fargues and Scholze developed the theory of solid sheaves with
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}
-coefficients in
[
FS24
, Chapter VII]
. They are subcategories of pro-étale sheaves of
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}
-modules that are small enough to afford some nice functorialities but large enough to be closed under all limits and colimits. Indeed, in the analytic setting, solid sheaves can be endowed with a so-called 5 functors formalism: the main feature is that any pullback functor admits a left adjoint and that arbitrary base change is true. It is however not possible to endow solid sheaves with a six functors formalism in this setting as the proper projection formula is not true in general (
[
FS24
, Warning VII.2.5]
).

The first step of this paper is to adapt the framework of solid sheaves to schemes. Let
X
be a quasi-compact and quasi-separated (qcqs) scheme, let
\mathcal{P}
be a subset of the set of prime numbers and let
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}=\prod_{p\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
If
j\colon U\to X
is a pro-étale map that can be written as a cofiltered limit of étale maps
j_{\alpha}\colon U_{\alpha}\to X
with each
U_{\alpha}
affine, we set

j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}\coloneqq\lim_{\alpha}(j_{\alpha})_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}.

The abelian category of
solid sheaves
on
X
is then defined as the localization
\mathrm{Sh}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
of
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
with respect to the natural maps
j_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}\to j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
. We then say that a complex of pro-étale sheaves of
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-modules is solid if its cohomology sheaves are solid. We denote by
\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
the full subcategory of
\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
spanned by solid objects. Finally, if
\Lambda
is a solid
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-algebra, we set

\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\coloneqq\mathrm{Mod}_{\Lambda}(\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}).

We prove that these categories have all the basic properties that one expects from a good category of solid sheaves.

Theorem A
.

(
1.2.8
,
1.3.2
,
1.3.11
and
1.3.12
) Let
X
be a qcqs scheme.

1.

The subcategory
\mathrm{Sh}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
of
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
is abelian and closed under extensions, small limits, colimits and base change. It is compactly generated by the pro-category of constructible étale sheaves of
\mathcal{P}
-torsion.

2.

Constructible complexes in the sense of
[
HRS23
]
are solid, i.e. we have an inclusion
\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})\subseteq\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
. This is also true with
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
-coefficients.

3.

Let
\Lambda
be a solid
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-algebra. The inclusion

\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\subseteq\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)

admits a left adjoint
(-)^{\blacksquare}
and is compatible with colimits and base change. There is a unique symmetric monoidal structure
\otimes^{\blacksquare}
on
\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
making the functor
(-)^{\blacksquare}
symmetric monoidal.

One of the main results of this paper is that, unlike in the analytic setting, the categories
\mathrm{D}(-,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
support a full six functor formalism over schemes on which all primes in
\mathcal{P}
are invertible (see
E
).

Pro-étale motives

We will construct the six-operations on solid sheaves by comparing them to a suitable category of pro-étale motives.
Note that if we know that
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
has the six functors, the rigidity theorem implies that over schemes such that
n
is invertible, the same holds for
\mathrm{D}((-)_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})
. We want to adapt this approach for solid sheaves.
The first step is to define a pro-étale version of motives and motivic spectra.

Let us now recall the (étale-local version of) Morel and Voevodsky’s construction of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant motivic spectra over a scheme
X
: we start from the category
\mathrm{Sm}_{X}
of smooth
X
-schemes endowed with the étale topology and build the category
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Sm}_{X},\mathrm{Sp})
of hypersheaves of spectra over
\mathrm{Sm}_{X}
; the category
\SH_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X)
of étale motivic spectra is then the
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-stabilization of the subcategory of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-local sheaves. For our purposes, the category of
\mathrm{Sm}_{X}
is too small: we want to replace the étale topology with the pro-étale topology.
To that end, we define the category
\mathrm{WSm}_{X}
of
weakly smooth

X
-schemes as the category of
X
-schemes whose structure morphism is a finite composition of smooth morphisms and weakly étale morphisms. As for smooth maps, the local structure of weakly smooth maps is simple: Zariski-locally on the source they can be written as a composition

Y\xrightarrow{f}\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X}\to X

with
f
weakly étale (this is
2.1.7
).
The category
\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
is then defined as the
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-stabilization of the subcategory
\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{S}^{1}}(X)
of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-local objects in the category
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\mathrm{Sp})
of pro-étale hypersheaves of spectra on
\mathrm{WSm}_{X}
.
The main result is that pro-étale motivic spectra afford the six functors in a slightly weaker form than the six functors on usual motivic spectra (we need finite presentation assumptions to get exceptional functors and not just the usual assumption that they are of finite type); furthermore étale motivic spectra embed into pro-étale motivic spectra under some finiteness assumptions:

Theorem B
.

(
2.5.6
and
2.6.2
)
We have a functor

\mathrm{SH}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\colon\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}})

that is part of a six functors formalism. More precisely,

1.

We have pairs of adjoint functors given by

•

For any morphism of schemes
f
we have an adjunction
(f^{*},f_{*})
with
f^{*}
the functor
\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(f)
.

•

For a finitely presented
f
between qcqs schemes, we have an adjunction
(f_{!},f^{!})
. There is a natural transformation
f_{!}\to f_{*}
which is an equivalence if
f
is furthermore proper.

•

Over any
X
, we have a closed monoidal structure
(\otimes_{X},\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{X})
on
\mathrm{SH}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
.

2.

Let
f\colon Y\to X
be a finitely presented morphism between qcqs schemes. Then the formation of
f_{!}
is compatible with pullbacks.

3.

For any finitely presented morphism
f\colon X\to Y
of qcqs schemes, we have a natural isomorphism

(f_{!}M)\otimes N\to f_{!}(M\otimes f^{*}N).

4.

In fact one can do better and obtain a six functors formalism as a lax symmetric monoidal functor on a category of correspondences that encode most of the wanted coherence of 1., 2. and 3. (see
2.5.4
).

5.

For any
f\colon X\to S
finitely presented smooth morphism of qcqs schemes, then with the notations of
2.4.4
, there is a natural equivalence
f^{!}\simeq\Sigma^{\Omega_{f}}\circ f^{*}
.

6.

For any closed immersion
i\colon F\to X
with qcqs open complement
j\colon X\setminus F\to X
, we have exact triangles of natural transformations

j_{!}j^{!}\to\mathrm{Id}\to i_{*}i^{*}

i_{!}i^{!}\to\mathrm{Id}\to j_{*}j^{*}

given by the unit and counit of the appropriate adjunctions.

Finally, if
X
is a locally étale bounded (
2.6.1
) qcqs scheme, then the canonical functor

\SH_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(S)\to\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(S)

is fully faithful.

Note in the above theorem we do in almost all statements need to assume that the morphisms in question are of finite presentation.
The reason that this is necessary lies within the sixth point, which is the crucial ingredient to prove all the other properties above.
For a non-finitely presented closed immersion of qcqs schemes, the open complement might not be quasi-compact and there are no localization sequences, see
2.3.5
.
This also explains the different behavior in the rigid analytic setting of
[
FS24
, §VII]
.
In analytic geometry it is rather rare for the open complement of a closed immersion to be quasi-compact and thus the arguments in this paper do not apply in that setting.

Condensed category theory

Let
\Lambda
be a condensed
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-algebra. The coefficient system
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(-,\Lambda)
of pro-étale motives with
\Lambda
-coefficients is then defined as the category of
H\Lambda
-modules in
\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
. By abstract nonsense, it also affords a six functor formalism.
Left Kan extension defines a functor

\mathrm{D}((-)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)\to\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(-,\Lambda).

As we have seen above this map can not be an equivalence since the domain does not have the six operations while the codomain does.
The idea is that in oder to fix this, we need to solidify the above map.
We have seen what solidifying the left-hand side means and we need to provide an analog of this procedure for the right-hand side. To that end, we use internal category theory as developed in
[
Mar21
,
MW24
,
MW22
]
.
The basic idea is that a
condensed
\infty
-category
is a sheaf of
\infty
-categories on the site of profinite sets and that a condensed functor is simply a map of sheaves. A condensed
\infty
-category is
presentable
if it is pointwise presentable and some squares are adjointable (see
1.4.3
, this in fact amounts to having colimits in a condensed sense). A condensed functor has a left or right adjoint whenever it has an adjoint pointwise and some squares are adjointable (see
1.4.5
, again this amounts to compatibility with condensed colimits). The category
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathrm{cond}}
is then defined as the category of presentable condensed categories with condensed left adjoint functors. As for the usual category
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}
, this category was endowed with a symmetric monoidal structure
-\otimes^{\mathrm{cond}}-
in
[
MW22
]
. The latter is a condensed version of the Lurie tensor product. Given a condensed category
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
, one can “forget the topology” and obtain the underlying category
\underline{\mathcal{C}}(*)
by taking global sections.

The basic examples of condensed
\infty
-categories that are used throughout the paper are

\underline{\mathrm{D}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},{\Lambda})\colon K\mapsto\mathrm{D}((X\times K)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)

and

\underline{\mathrm{Solid}}_{\Lambda}\colon K\mapsto\mathrm{D}(K,{\Lambda})^{\blacksquare},

where
K
ranges through the category of profinite sets. We also set

\underline{\mathrm{Mod}}_{\Lambda}\coloneqq\underline{\mathrm{D}}(*_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},{\Lambda})
. The
solidification
of a
\underline{\mathrm{Mod}}_{\Lambda}
-linear condensed presentable
\infty
-category
\underline{\mathcal{C}}
is then defined as
\underline{\mathcal{C}}^{\blacksquare}\coloneqq\underline{\mathcal{C}}\otimes^{\mathrm{cond}}_{\underline{\mathrm{Mod}}_{\Lambda}}\underline{\mathrm{Solid}}_{\Lambda}
. We can recover solid sheaves on schemes using these tools:

Proposition C
.

(
1.4.17
)
Let
X
be a qcqs scheme. Then

\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}=\underline{\mathrm{D}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}(*).

The rigidity theorem

We are now armed to prove the rigidity theorem. For it to be true, we assume all primes in
\mathcal{P}
to be invertible on our schemes, because otherwise étale cohomology may not be
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant. The most natural idea is to look at the condensed presentable
\infty
-category defined by

\underline{\mathrm{DM}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,\Lambda)\colon K\mapsto\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X\times K,\Lambda),

to solidify it and take global sections. This yields a presentable
\infty
-category that we denote by
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1},{\mathbb{G}_{m}}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
.
1
1

1

Note that since the abstract solidification we introduced above is only defined for coefficients in a
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-algebra, we do not yet have a good category of solid motives integrally. Our methods also don’t immediately generalize to the setting of integral coefficients: The main obstacle is that
1.2.7
does not hold integrally. We plan to further address this point in future work.

We automatically get a map

\mathrm{D}(-,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1},{\mathbb{G}_{m}}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{(-)},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}

that we expect to be an equivalence.
At the moment however we do not know how to prove this.
The main obstacle is that we do not know if the unit in the codomain is complete.
To circumvent this issue, we further localize this category. Let us first take a step back and start from the effective case. We let
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)
be the category of
\Lambda
-modules in
\mathrm{SH}^{S^{1}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
; we naturally get a condensed presentably monoidal
\infty
-category

\underline{\mathrm{D}}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)\colon K\mapsto\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X\times K},\Lambda)

The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
of
solid
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant sheaves
over
X
is then defined as
\underline{\mathrm{D}}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}(*).
The following result essentially amounts showing that solid sheaves are homotopy invariant which we bootstrap from the torsion étale case:

Theorem D
.

(
3.2.6
)
Assume that each prime number in
\mathcal{P}
is invertible on
X
. The functor

\rho_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\colon\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}

obtained by Kan extension along the inclusion
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\to\mathrm{WSm}_{X}
followed by
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-localization is fully faithful, commutes with all small limits and colimits, all pullbacks and with
f_{\sharp}
for
f
weakly étale.

Observe now that if
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}(1)
is the free sheaf over the pointed scheme
({\mathbb{G}_{m}}_{X},1)
, we have a natural map
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}(1)\to\lim_{n\in\Pi\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}(1)\simeq\mu_{\infty,\mathcal{P}}
where
\Pi\mathcal{P}
is the set of those
n
which are only divisible by primes in
\mathcal{P}
and with

\mu_{\infty,\mathcal{P}}\coloneqq\lim_{n\in\Pi\mathcal{P}}\mu_{n}
. The usual torsion rigidity theorem implies that torsion étale sheaves are local with respect to this map. Thus, so are pro-(torsion étale) sheaves. A bit more work shows that both the source and the target of this map are compact (see the proof of
3.3.7
) and thus we see that
\rho_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}
lands in the subcategory of local objects. This is a very good sign as
\mu_{\infty,\mathcal{P}}
is
\otimes
-invertible, so that localizing with respect to this map also enforces Tate-stability. This motivates the following definitions: we let
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{eff}}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
(resp.
\mathrm{DM}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
) be the localization of
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
(resp.
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1},{\mathbb{G}_{m}}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
) at the maps

M(1)\to M\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}}\mu_{\infty,\mathcal{P}}

for all
M
. We can now finally state our rigidity result:

Theorem E
.

(
3.3.7
and
3.3.9
)
Assume that each prime number in
\mathcal{P}
is invertible on
X
.
The functors

\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1},{\mathbb{G}_{m}}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}

induce equivalences

\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{eff}}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{DM}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}.

In particular, the
\mathrm{D}(-,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
are endowed with the six functors on schemes
X
such that each prime number in
\mathcal{P}
is invertible on
X
. If
R
is a commutative (discrete) ring, this yields a
solid
\mathcal{P}
-adic realization functor
:

\rho_{\blacksquare}\colon\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,R)\to\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,R\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})\to\mathrm{DM}(X,R\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}\simeq\mathrm{D}(X,R\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}

We can then compare our realization with the classical
\ell
-adic realization.

Proposition F
.

(
3.3.12
)
We have a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})^{\blacksquare}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Q})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(X,\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})^{\blacksquare}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\blacksquare}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes\mathbb{Q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes\mathbb{Q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\blacksquare}}

of symmetric monoidal left adjoints which restricts to the classical square

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{0.11777pt}{-24.21637pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

where
\rho_{\ell}
is the
\ell
-adic realization functor of
[
CD16
]
and
\rho_{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}}
is the
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
-adic realization functor of
[
RS20
, 2.1.2]
.

Over schemes of characteristic zero, the above result also holds for
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}
and the ring of finite adeles
\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q},f}
instead of
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
. This allows for another definition of the algebra
\mathscr{N}\in\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q}),\mathbb{Z})
such that modules over it are Nori motives, as considered in
[
RT24b
]
. In forthcoming work, we plan to study a spectral analogue of the constructions of this paper. This would lead to a definition of spectral Nori motives.

Notations and Conventions

We denote by
\mathrm{Ani}
the category of anima
2
2

2

also known as spaces,
\infty
-groupoids, Kan complexes, sets…
.
Let
\mathcal{C}
be a site.
We denote by
\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}(\mathcal{C})
(resp.
\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{C})
) the category of presheaves (resp. hypersheaves) of anima over
\mathcal{C}
.
We let
\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{C},\mathrm{Sets})
be the full subcategory of sheaves of sets.
If
R
is a ring in
\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{C},\mathrm{Sets})
, we let
\mathrm{Sh}(\mathcal{C},R)
be the category of
R
-modules. When dealing with presheaves on a site with values in a
\infty
-category, we will always use hypersheaves and never sheaves.
For the sake of readability, we will sometime drop the "
\infty
" and simply refer to
\infty
-categories as categories (and the same for their condensed analogue defined in
1.4.1
).
Finally, all schemes we consider are qcqs.

1 Solid sheaves on schemes.

In this section, we adapt
[
FS24
, Sections VII.1 & VII.2]
to schemes.
We begin by recalling a few preliminaries on pro-étale sheaves in
Section

1.1
.
In
Section

1.2
we define the abelian category of solid pro-étale sheaves and prove its main properties.
Then, in
Section

1.3
we study the derived category of solid sheaves on a scheme.
Both of these sections closely follow the material in
[
FS24
, §VII]
.
Finally in
Section

1.4
we use ideas from condensed category theory to provide a different perspective on solid sheaves.
This approach will be used in
Section

3
to solidify the categories of pro-étale motives constructed in the
Section

2
.

1.1 Recollections on the pro-étale topos

If
X
is a scheme, we let
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
denote the small pro-étale site of
X
consisting of weakly étale
X
-schemes and by
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}^{\operatorname{\mathrm{aff}}}
the subcategory made of cofiltered limits with affine transition maps of étale
X
-schemes which are also affine as schemes. Recall from
[
BS15
, §2]
that an affine scheme is said to be
w
-contractible
if any pro-étale cover of it admits a section. It is said to be
w
-local
if any open cover splits and the subset of its closed points is closed. We often use
[
BS15
, Theorem 1.8]
: an affine scheme is
w
-contractible if and only if it is
w
-local, all of its local rings at
closed points are strictly henselian, and its
\pi_{0}
is an extremally disconnected profinite set.
Finally, a morphism of
w
-local affine schemes is
w
-local
if it maps closed points to closed points.

1.1.1
.

Let
X=\operatorname{Spec}(R)
be an affine scheme and
i\colon Z=\operatorname{Spec}(R/I)\rightarrow X
be a closed immersion.
Recall that by
[
BS15
, Lemma 2.2.12]
the base change functor

-\otimes_{R}R/I\colon\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}(R_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})\rightarrow\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}((R/I)_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})

admits a fully faithful right adjoint

\operatorname{Hens}_{R}(-)\colon\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}((R/I)_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})\rightarrow\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}(R_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}).

As in loc. cit. we often simplify notation a bit and simply write

\displaystyle\widetilde{(-)}\colon Z_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}^{\operatorname{aff}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}^{\operatorname{aff}}

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle\mapsto\widetilde{V}

for the corresponding functor on schemes (that we also denote by
\mathrm{Hens}_{X}
).
This functor is fully faithful and left adjoint to the base-change functor.
It follows that for a pro-étale sheaf
F
on
X
, the sheaf
i^{*}F
may be described as the sheaf associated to the presheaf

V\mapsto F(\widetilde{V}).

Proposition 1.1.2
.

Let
W
be a
w
-contractible affine scheme and let
i\colon W^{c}\to W
be the inclusion of its subscheme of closed points.
Then
\mathrm{Hens}_{W}(W^{c})=W
.

Proof.

Let us write
\widetilde{W^{c}}=\mathrm{Hens}_{W}(W^{c})
. First recall that
W^{c}
is
w
-contractible: it is
w
-local by
[
BS15
, Lemma 2.1.3]
, its local rings are algebraically closed field and its
\pi_{0}
is the same as that of
W
.
By full faithfulness of the henselisation functor, we have
\widetilde{W^{c}}\times_{W}W^{c}=W^{c}
, whence a map
W^{c}\to\widetilde{W^{c}}
. Given a pro-étale cover
W^{\prime}\to\widetilde{W^{c}}
,
its pullback
W^{\prime}\times_{\widetilde{W^{c}}}W^{c}\to W^{c}
splits. As
W^{\prime}\times_{\widetilde{W^{c}}}W^{c}=W^{\prime}\times_{\widetilde{W^{c}}}(\widetilde{W^{c}}\times_{W}W^{c})=W^{\prime}\times_{W}W^{c}
, the splitting yields a map
\widetilde{W^{c}}\to W^{\prime}
by adjunction. It is a splitting of our pro-étale cover (by adjunction again).
Thus
\widetilde{W^{c}}
is also
w
-contractible.

Furthermore the canonical map
W^{c}\to\widetilde{W^{c}}
is a closed immersion as it is the pullback of
W^{c}\to W
along the map
\widetilde{W^{c}}\to W
and
W^{c}\to\widetilde{W^{c}}
is a henselian pair (indeed, this follows from the universal property of
\mathrm{Hens}_{W}
together with
[
Aut18
,
Tag 09XI (2)
]
).
We claim that
W^{c}\to\widetilde{W^{c}}
is exactly the subscheme of closed points.
Indeed, by
[
HRS23
, Corollary 4.4]
, it induces an isomorphism on
\pi_{0}
and the claim follows because any connected component of
\widetilde{W^{c}}
has a unique closed point since
\widetilde{W^{c}}
is
w
-local.
Since the canonical map

\widetilde{W^{c}}\to W

is pro-étale,
w
-local and induces an isomorphism on closed points, it follows from the proof of
[
BS15
, lemma 2.3.8]
that it is an isomorphism.
∎

Remark 1.1.3

(Size issues)
.

Since the category of weakly étale
X
-schemes is not small this induces some set-theoretic issues.
In the end, one can always circumvent these issues and they do not have any serious effect on our results.
For the more cautious reader, we suggest one of the following two ways of reading this paper:

1.

Fix once and for all two strongly inaccessible cardinals
\delta<\epsilon
.
All schemes are then assumed to be
\delta
-small and all categorical constructions, such as taking sheaves on a site, are taken with respect to the larger universe determined by
\epsilon
.
In particular
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})
always means hypersheaves of
\epsilon
-small anima on
\delta
-small weakly étale
X
-schemes, and similarly for the
\infty
-category of condensed anima
\Cond(\mathrm{Ani})
.

2.

If the reader does not want to work with universes, they may proceed as follows.
For a scheme
X
, choose a strong limit cardinal
\kappa
such that
X
is
\kappa
-small.
Write
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\kappa}
for the category of
\kappa
-small weakly étale
X
-schemes.
The assumption that
\kappa
is a strong limit cardinal guarantees that there are enough
w
-contractible schemes in
X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\kappa}
.
We then define

\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})\coloneqq\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{\kappa}\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\kappa})

and similarly for the category of condensed anima.
This is the approach taken by Clausen and Scholze in
[
CS21
]
.

Lemma 1.1.4
.

Let
X
be a quasi-compact scheme.
Then there is a surjective morphism of schemes
f\colon E\to X
where
E
is reduced,
0
-dimensional, affine and all its local rings are separably closed fields.
Furthermore for a such a scheme
E
we have a canonical equivalence

\mathrm{Sh}(E_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})\simeq\Cond(\mathrm{Ani})_{/\pi_{0}(E)}.

Proof.

Since
X
is quasi-compact, we can assume
X
to be affine and reduced. By
[
BS15
, Remark 2.1.11]
there exist maps

(X^{Z})^{c}\xrightarrow{a}X^{Z}\xrightarrow{b}X

with
b
a pro-open covering and
a
a closed immersion, with
X^{Z}
a
w
-local scheme and
(X^{Z})^{c}
its subscheme of closed points. In particular by
[
BS15
, Lemma 2.2.3]
the scheme
(X^{Z})^{c}
is absolutely flat (
i.e.
reduced and
0
-dimensional).

The existence of
E
then follows from
[
BS15
, Lemma 2.2.7]
. Now, the pro-étale site of
E
is the same as the pro-Zariski site of
E
by
[
BS15
, Lemma 2.3.8]
and as
E
is
0
-dimensional, the map
E\to\pi_{0}(E)
is a homeomorphism so that the lemma follows.
∎

Corollary 1.1.5
.

Let
W
be a
w
-contractible scheme and let
i\colon W^{c}\to W
be the inclusion of its subscheme of closed points.
Then for any
F\in\mathrm{Sh}(W_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})
the canonical map

\eta\colon i^{*}F(W^{c})\to F(W)

is an equivalence.

Proof.

By
1.1.2
, the map
\eta
is an equivalence if
F
is representable by a pro-affine-étale
W
-scheme.
Since
i^{*}
,
\Gamma(W^{c},-)
and
\Gamma(W,-)
preserves sifted colimits, and an arbitrary pro-étale sheaf is a sifted colimit of pro-affine-étale representables, the claim follows.
∎

Lemma 1.1.6
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a surjective morphism of schemes.
Then the pullback functor
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{Sh}(Y_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})\to\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})
is conservative.

Proof.

We may immediately reduce to the case where both
X
and
Y
are affine.
Since
w
-contractible schemes form a basis for the topology the collection of functors given by evaluating at any
w
-contractible
W
is conservative.
Using
1.1.5
it follows that the collection of functors given by pulling back along the maps

W^{c}\to W\to X

for any
w
-contractible
W
is conservative.
Hence, we may assume that
W^{c}=Y
to reduce to the case where
Y
is reduced of Krull dimension 0 with separably closed residue fields.
By
1.1.4
, we may choose another such scheme
E
with a surjection
E\to X
to reduce to the case
X=E
.
Again by
1.1.4
the functor
f^{*}
identifies with the functor

\Cond(\mathrm{Ani})_{/\pi_{0}(X)}\to\Cond(\mathrm{Ani})_{/\pi_{0}(Y)}

induced by pulling back along
\pi_{0}(f)\colon\pi_{0}(X)\to\pi_{0}(Y)
.
But by construction the map
\pi_{0}(f)
is surjective and thus an effective epimorphim in condensed anima and so pulling back along it is conservative.
∎

Lemma 1.1.7
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a morphism of schemes.
Then the pullback functor
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{Sh}(Y_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})\to\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})
is compatible with limits.

Proof.

This is
[
Wol22
, Corollary 4.18]
.
∎

1.2 Abelian categories.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme. We fix a set
\mathcal{P}
of prime numbers in this section. Recall the ring
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
from the introduction. The ring
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
is a topological ring in the obvious way and thus we may consider it as a condensed ring.
Via the canonical morphism of topoi

\Cond(\rm{Set})\to\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\rm{Set}),

see for example
[
HHL+25
, Remark 3.5]
, we may therefore view
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
as a ring object in
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\rm{Set})
.
We write
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})=\mathrm{Mod}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}}(\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\rm{Ab}))
and
\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
for the derived category of this abelian category.

Recollection 1.2.1
.

Recall that by
[
sga72
, Exposé VII, Théorème 5.7]
we have a fully faithful map:

\mathrm{Pro}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{aff},{\fp}})\to X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}

where the left-hand side denotes the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Pro}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})
spanned by cofiltered limits of finite presentation étale
X
-schemes which are affine. Moreover, this induces an equivalence between the associated topoi by
[
BS15
, Theorem 2.3.4]
.

If
j\colon U\to X
is a pro-étale map, the pullback
j^{*}
on pro-étale sheaves has a left adjoint that we denote by
j_{\sharp}
.

Definition 1.2.2
.

If
j\colon U\to X
is a pro-étale map that can be written as a cofiltered limit of étale maps
j_{\alpha}\colon U_{\alpha}\to X
with each
U_{\alpha}
affine, we set

j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}\coloneqq\lim(j_{\alpha})_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}\in\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}).

We have a map
j_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}\to j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
.

Definition 1.2.3
.

Let
F
be an abelian pro-étale sheaf of
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-modules. We say that
F
is
solid
, if for every
j
as in
1.2.2
, the natural map

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}},F)\to\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(j_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}},F)=F(U)

is an equivalence.
We denote by
\mathrm{Sh}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
spanned by solid objects.

Finally, we say that a complex of pro-étale sheaves of
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-modules is
solid
if its cohomology sheaves are solid. We denote by
\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
the full subcategory of
\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
spanned by the solid objects.

Remark 1.2.4
.

Equivalently
\mathrm{Sh}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
is the reflective localization at the maps
j_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}\to j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
.
In particular the inclusion
\mathrm{Sh}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
admits a left adjoint that we denote by
(-)^{\blacksquare}
and call the
solidification functor
.

We now want to understand the category of solid sheaves. Our main result is Theorem
1.2.8
and to that end, we first need a few technical lemmas.

1.2.5
.

Recall that an étale sheaf of sets
F
is
constructible
if there is a stratification of
X
by quasi-compact locally closed subschemes such that
F
is locally constant with finite stalks over each stratum; we let
X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{cons}}
be the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathrm{Sets})
made of constructible étale sheaves. We denote by
\nu\colon\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathrm{Sets})\to\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathrm{Sets})
the morphism of sites induced by the inclusion and by
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}[-]
the left adjoint to the forgetful functor
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})\to\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathrm{Sets})
.

In many reductions, we need the following result on schemes.

Lemma 1.2.6
.

Let
F
be a constructible étale sheaf of sets. Then in
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
the map

\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}[\nu^{*}F]\to\lim_{n\in\Pi\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}[\nu^{*}F],

where
\Pi\mathcal{P}
is the set of positive integers, ordered by divisibility, that can be written as products of primes in
\mathcal{P}
, is an equivalence.

Proof.

We can assume that
F
is étale-locally constant: indeed by
[
BS15
, Lemma 6.1.11]
, given a finite stratification of
X
with quasi-compact strata, the family of restrictions to the strata is jointly conservative and these restrictions commute with limits by Lemma
1.1.7
.

Take a surjective map
g\colon E\to X
with
E
affine reduced of Krull dimension
0
with separably closed residue fields using
1.1.4
.
Since
g^{*}
is conservative and commutes with limits by Lemma
1.1.6
and Lemma
1.1.7
, we may replace
X
by
E
.

In that case, the Zariski and the étale site coincide by
[
Sch17
, Corollary 2.5]
and thus
F
is Zariski-locally constant with finite stalks.
Thus we may assume it is actually constant, in which case the claim is obvious, since cofiltered limits commute with finite sums.
∎

An étale sheaf of abelian groups is said to be
\mathcal{P}
-torsion constructible
if it is
\mathcal{P}
-torsion and constructible as an étale sheaf of sets. We let
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})
be the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z})
made of
\mathcal{P}
-torsion constructible étale sheaves.

We have the following analogue of
[
FS24
, Proposition VII.1.6]
in our setting:

Lemma 1.2.7
.

Let
(F_{i})
be a cofiltered inverse system of

\mathcal{P}
-torsion constructible étale sheaves. Then for all
q>0
, the
R^{q}\lim F_{i}
vanishes when taken in the category
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
.

Proof.

It suffices to prove that for any
w
-contractible weakly étale
X
-scheme
W
and any
q
, the abelian group
[R^{q}\lim F_{i}](W)
vanishes.
By
1.1.5
and
1.1.7
, we may replace
X
by the set of closed points
W^{c}
of
W
and therefore assume that
X
is affine reduced of Krull dimension
0
with separably closed residue fields.
By
[
Sch17
, Corollary 2.5]
, we get that
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})=\mathrm{Sh}(|X|)
where
|X|
the underlying topological space of
X
which is extremally disconnected.
Now the same proof as in the end of
[
FS24
, Proposition VII.1.6]
applies.
∎

Theorem 1.2.8
.

Keep the above notations.

1.

The subcategory
\mathrm{Sh}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
of
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
is weak Serre and closed under small limits and colimits. For
j\colon U\to X
as above, the object
j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
is the solidification of
j_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
. The collection of the
j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
generates the abelian category
\mathrm{Sh}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
.

2.

Let
F
be an object of
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
. The following are equivalent

(a)

The sheaf
F
is compact in
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
and solid.

(b)

The sheaf
F
is solid and compact in
\mathrm{Sh}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
.

(c)

The sheaf
F
can be written as a cofiltered limit of torsion constructible étale sheaves.

3.

The subcategory of
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
spanned by objects satisfying the above equivalent conditions is weak Serre. As an abelian category it is equivalent to the pro-category
\mathrm{Pro}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}))
.

4.

The category
\mathrm{Sh}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
is equivalent to
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}(\mathrm{Pro}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})))
.

5.

Let
K
be a complex of pro-étale sheaves of
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-modules.
Then, it is solid if and only if the natural maps

\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}},K)\to j_{*}j^{*}K

are equivalences for any
(j\colon U=\lim_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}\to X)\in\mathrm{Pro}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{aff},{\fp}})
.

Proof.

We mostly follow the proof of
[
FS24
, Theorem VII.1.3]
.
The category
\mathrm{Pro}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}))
is abelian by
[
KS06
, Proposition 8.6.7]
. Furthermore,
1.2.7
ensures that the functor

\mathrm{Pro}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}))\to\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})

(1)

is exact. We want to show that it is fully faithful. Let
(F_{i})
be a cofiltered inverse system of

\mathcal{P}
-torsion constructible étale sheaves and let
G
be a
\mathcal{P}
-torsion constructible étale sheaf, we have to prove that the natural map:

\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z})}(F_{i},G)\to\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(\lim\nu^{*}F_{i},\nu^{*}G)

is an equivalence.

Recall the category
X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{cons}}
of set-valued constructible étale sheaves.
We consider the category
\mathrm{Sh}(\mathrm{Pro}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{cons}}),\mathrm{Sets})
of sheaves on
\mathrm{Pro}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{cons}})
for the coherent topology,
[
Lurb
, Remark 7.1.1, Definition B.5.3]
.
By
[
Lurb
, Example 7.1.7]
the natural map

\mathrm{Pro}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{cons}})\to\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathrm{Sets})

induces an equivalence
\mathrm{Sh}(\mathrm{Pro}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{cons}}),\mathrm{Sets})\to\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathrm{Sets})
by Kan extension.
In particular, it is fully faithful and therefore, the map

\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathrm{Sets})}(F_{i},G)\to\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathrm{Sets})}(\lim\nu^{*}F_{i},\nu^{*}G)

is an equivalence, since the coherent topology is subcanonical
[
Lurb
, Corollary B.5.6]
.
Enforcing compatibility with addition on the right hand side amounts to saying that two different maps
\lim\nu^{*}F_{i}\times\lim\nu^{*}F_{i}\to G
coincide (and similarly on the left hand side); as before, we have

\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathrm{Sets})}(F_{i}\times F_{i},G)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathrm{Sets})}(\lim\nu^{*}F_{i}\times\lim\nu^{*}F_{i},\nu^{*}G)

and taking equalizers, we get

\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z})}(F_{i},G)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z})}(\lim\nu^{*}F_{i},\nu^{*}G).

Furthermore, any map between cofiltered limits of constructible
\mathcal{P}
-torsion sheaves that is
\mathbb{Z}
-linear is
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-linear.
Hence, the category
\mathrm{Pro}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}))
is equivalent to an abelian subcategory of
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
that we denote by
\mathcal{C}(X)
.

By definition, the category
\mathcal{C}(X)
is spanned by the objects that satisfy condition (c). We now show that its objects are solid. It suffices to prove that
\mathcal{P}
-torsion constructible sheaves are solid since solid objects are closed under limits. Let
F
be a
\mathcal{P}
-torsion constructible étale sheaf and let
(U_{\alpha})
be a cofiltered system of étale
X
-schemes which are affine. Denote by
j\colon\lim_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}\to X
the canonical pro-étale map. In the commutative diagram

\lxSVG@closescope {

\lxSVG@closescope {

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

the right vertical arrow is invertible by the Yoneda lemma,
the bottom horizontal arrow is invertible because of
[
BS15
, Lemma 5.2]
,
and one sees that the left vertical arrow is invertible by using
1.2.6
to write
j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
as an object of
\mathcal{C}(X)
, and the fact that
Equation

1
is fully faithful. Thus, the top horizontal arrow is an equivalence and this proves that
\nu^{*}F
is solid. Hence
\mathcal{C}(X)
is contained in
\mathrm{Sh}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
.

We now prove that the objects of
\mathcal{C}(X)
are also compact in
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
. First note that the objects of
\mathrm{Pro}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{cons}})
are compact in
\mathrm{Sh}(\mathrm{Pro}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{cons}}),\mathrm{Sets})\simeq\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathrm{Sets})
; this is because filtered colimits of sheaves are computed in the category of set-valued presheaves over
\mathrm{Pro}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{cons}})
by
[
Lurb
, Remark 7.1.1]
. Let
F
be in
\mathcal{C}(X)
and let
G
be a pro-étale sheaf of
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-modules.
As above, one can describe
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(F,G)
as the subset of
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})}(F,G)
made of maps satisfying additivity and
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-linearity,
i.e
certain maps
F\times F\to G
and
F\times\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}\to G
agree; as the functor
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})}(H,-)
is compatible with filtered colimits for any
H
in
\mathcal{C}(X)
, this description also commutes with filtered colimits and therefore
F
is compact in
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
. Hence, we proved that condition (c) implies condition (a).

We can also deduce that solid objects are closed under filtered colimits: indeed, if
j\colon U\to X
is a pro-étale map that can be written as a cofiltered limit of étale maps from affine schemes, the sheaf
j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
is compact in
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
as we already know that it belongs to
\mathcal{C}(X)
. This also proves that condition (a) implies condition (b).

We now prove 4.
i.e.
, we describe the category of solid sheaves.
Since solid objects are closed under filtered colimits, we have a functor

\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{C}(X)\to\mathrm{Sh}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}.

As all objects of
\mathcal{C}(X)
are compact, it is fully faithful.
It is also an exact functor of abelian categories by
[
KS06
, Proposition 8.6.7]
.

As
j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
is in
\mathcal{C}(X)
, it is solid. Thus, the Yoneda lemma and the definition of solid objects yield

(j_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}\xrightarrow{\sim}j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}.

Thus any solid sheaf
F
admits a surjection from a direct sum of
j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
for various
j
as before.
As the kernel is also solid, we get a presentation

\bigoplus(j_{\beta})_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}\to\bigoplus(j_{\gamma})_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}\to F\to 0

which implies that
F
belongs to
\operatorname{\mathrm{Ind}}\mathcal{C}(X)
, since the latter is closed under cofibers.
From this, we also deduce that condition (b) implies condition (c) because
\mathcal{C}(X)
is idempotent complete and using that taking compact objects of the Ind-category is the same as taking the idempotent-completion by
[
Lur09
, Lemma 5.4.2.4]
. Thus we proved 2.

So far we proved 2. and 4., and everything in points 1. and 3. except for the stabilities under extensions.
This follows from 5., which we now prove.
We begin by showing that any solid complex
K
satisfies the condition of 5.
By dévissage along the Postnikov tower (note that the formula commutes with limits in
K
as
j^{*}
commutes with limits by
1.1.7
) and a spectral sequence argument, we can reduce to
K=F[0]
being in the heart.
The strategy is as follows: we prove that the equation of 5. commutes with filtered colimits so that we may assume that
F
is a constructible torsion étale sheaf, and then we prove the claim for those specific sheaves. Both steps use Breen-Deligne resolutions:

By
[
HRS23
, Lemma 5.3]
the right-hand side of 5. commutes with filtered colimits of objects in the heart.
We claim that the left-hand side
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}},-)
also commutes with filtered colimits in
\mathrm{Sh}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
.
To prove this claim, note that the
p_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
for
p\colon V\to X
weakly étale and
V

w
-contractible are compact generators of
\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
(compactness follows from
[
HRS23
, Lemma 4.1]
) and therefore, it suffices to show that the
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(p_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}},\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}},-))
are compatible with filtered colimits.
The latter coincides with
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(p_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}},-)
.
Note that
p_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
is the non-derived tensor product because
p_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
is projective.
We now use a Breen-Deligne resolution of
j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
which implies as in
[
CS21
, Corollary 4.8]
that we have a functorial converging spectral sequence
E_{*}^{p,q}(j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}^{r_{p,i}}\times V,-)
whose first page is of the form

E_{1}^{p,q}=\prod_{i=1}^{n_{p}}\mathrm{Ext}^{q}_{\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}[j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}^{r_{p,i}}\times V],-)\Rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{p+q}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(p_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}},-),

with
n_{p}
and
r_{p,i}
some universal constants.
Note that the sheaf
j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}^{r_{p,i}}\times V
is a cofiltered limit of affine étale
X
-schemes by
1.2.6
.
Thus, the left-hand side commutes with filtered colimits by
[
Aut18
,
Tag 0739
]
and thus the abutment also commutes with filtered colimits, which proves the claim.
Hence, we can assume that
F
is compact.
We can then further reduce to
F
being a
\mathcal{P}
-torsion constructible étale sheaf as both sides of 5. commute with limits (which are derived in our case by
1.2.7
).

We now will prove that 5. is an equivalence for
K=F[0]
and
F
a
\mathcal{P}
-torsion constructible étale sheaf. Again it suffices to check that the map is an equivalence after applying
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(p_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}},-)
for all
p\colon V\to X
pro-étale affine with
V

w
-contractible. Note that the right hand-side of 5. then becomes
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(p_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes j_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}},F[0])
by using adjunctions and the projection formula for
j_{\sharp}
. Using again Breen-Deligne resolutions to get simultaneous resolutions of
j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
and
(j_{\alpha})_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
we obtain as above a map

\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{\alpha}E_{*}^{p,q}((j_{\alpha})_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}^{r_{p,i}}\times V,F)\to E_{*}^{p,q}(j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}^{r_{p,i}}\times V,F)

of spectral sequences which on the
E_{1}
-page is of the form

\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{\alpha}\prod_{i=1}^{n_{p}}\mathrm{Ext}^{q}_{\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}[(j_{\alpha})_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}^{r_{p,i}}\times V],F)\to\prod_{i=1}^{n_{p}}\mathrm{Ext}^{q}_{\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}[j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}^{r_{p,i}}\times V],F).

This map is an equivalence for all
p,q\in\mathbb{Z}
. Indeed, since
j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}^{r_{q}}\times V=\lim_{\alpha}(j_{\alpha})_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}^{r_{q}}\times V
, this follows from
[
BS15
, Corollary 5.1.6]
because the
j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}^{r_{q}}\times V
and
(j_{\alpha})_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}^{r_{q}}\times V
are all limits of affine schemes by
1.2.6
. As the two spectral sequences are equivalent, so are their abutments, thus the map

\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{\alpha}\mathrm{H}^{n}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(p_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes(j_{\alpha})_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}},F)\to\mathrm{H}^{n}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(p_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}},F)

is an equivalence.
Using the projection formula for
(j_{\alpha})_{\sharp}
, the left-hand side can be written

\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{\alpha}\mathrm{H}^{n}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}((u_{\alpha})_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}},F)\simeq\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{\alpha}\mathrm{H}^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(W_{\alpha},F_{\mid W_{\alpha}})

with the notation of the following cartesian diagram:
