\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

By
[
Aut18
,
Theorem 09YQ
]
we have

\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{\alpha}\mathrm{H}^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(W_{\alpha},F_{\mid W_{\alpha}})\simeq\mathrm{H}^{n}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(W,F_{\mid W})

with
W
the base change of
p
along
j
, thus doing the previous operations in reverse we find

\mathrm{H}^{n}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(p_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}},F)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{H}^{n}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(p_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes j_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}},F)

which is what we wanted to prove.

We now prove the converse of 5.
Let us write
\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{L\blacksquare}
for the full subcategory spanned by those complexes of pro-étale sheaves of
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-modules
K
such that for every
j
as before, the map

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}},K)\to\Gamma(U,K)

is an equivalence.
Clearly all complexes satisfying the conclusion of 5. are in
\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{L\blacksquare}
.
In particular we have
\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}\subseteq\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{L\blacksquare}
by what we have just shown.
To show that they agree we observe that the inclusion
\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{L\blacksquare}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
admits a left adjoint
L
.
Note that
D(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
is closed under colimits in
\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
.
Thus we are done if we prove that the natural map
L(j_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})\to j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
is an equivalence, since the latter is contained in
D(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
.
Since
j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
is in
\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{L\blacksquare}
it suffices to show that for any
K\in\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{L\blacksquare}
the canonical map

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{L\blacksquare}}(j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}},K)\to\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(j_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}},K)

is an equivalence, which is true by definition.
∎

Corollary 1.2.9
.

If
F
is an abelian étale sheaf of
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
-modules on
X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
with
n\in\Pi\mathcal{P}
(see
1.2.6
for this notation), the sheaf
\nu^{*}F
is solid.

Proof.

1.2.8
shows that this is true if
F
is constructible. As solid sheaves are closed under colimits and
\nu^{*}
is compatible with colimits, the result is true because any sheaf of
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
-modules is a colimit of constructible sheaves by
[
Aut18
,
Lemma 03SA
]
.
∎

Remark 1.2.10
.

In
1.2.2
we have chosen to work with inverse limits of affine étale
X
-schemes.
We could have also used other generators of the pro-étale site instead.
To see this we need to make some preliminary observations. We consider the functor

\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}[-]\colon X^{\rm{cons}}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to\mathrm{Pro}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}))

sending a constructible sheaf of sets
\mathcal{F}
to the free
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-module on it.
Note that this functor does indeed land in the full subcategory
\mathrm{Pro}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}))\subseteq\mathrm{Sh}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
by
1.2.6
.
Furthermore it preserves finite colimits.
Since cofiltered limits commute with finite colimits in
\mathrm{Pro}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}))
, it follows that the extension to pro-objects

\rm{Pro}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}[-])\colon\rm{Pro}(X^{\rm{cons}}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})\to\mathrm{Pro}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}))

also commutes with finite colimits.
Now by
1.2.7
the obvious functor

\mathrm{Pro}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}))\to\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})

is exact so in particular it also preserves finite colimits.
It follows that the composite

\rm{Pro}(X^{\rm{cons}}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})\xrightarrow{\rm{Pro}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}[-])}\mathrm{Pro}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}))\to\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})

is exact as well.

Now observe that if
\mathcal{F}=\lim_{i}\mathcal{F}_{i}\in\rm{Pro}(X^{\rm{cons}}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})
this functor, by definition sends
\mathcal{F}
to
\lim_{i}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}[\mathcal{F}_{i}]
.
In particular if
\mathcal{F}
is represented by some
j\colon U\to X
in
\mathrm{Pro}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{aff},{\fp}})
, it agrees with
j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
.
It is a consequence of
[
Lurb
, Remark 7.1.7]
that all objects in
\rm{Pro}(X^{\rm{cons}}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})
are qcqs objects of the topos
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\rm{Set})
.
In particular we may write them as a finite colimit of representables
j\colon U\to X
in
\mathrm{Pro}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{aff},{\fp}})
.
This shows that the canonical comparison map

\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}[\mathcal{F}]^{\blacksquare}\to\lim_{i}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}[\mathcal{F}_{i}]\in\mathrm{Sh}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}

writes as a finite colimits of maps of the form
(j_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}\to j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
and is thus an equivalence.
This shows that the full subcategory
\mathrm{Sh}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}\subseteq\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
is equivalently the reflective localization at the larger class of maps of the form

\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}[\mathcal{F}]\to\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}[\mathcal{F}]^{\blacksquare}\simeq\lim_{i}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}[\mathcal{F}_{i}].

The above considerations also show that we could have worked with a smaller class of maps instead.
Since
w
-contractible schemes which are in
\mathrm{Pro}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{\operatorname{\mathrm{aff}},\fp})
also generate the topos
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\rm{Set})
under colimits, the same arguments as above show that
\mathrm{Sh}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
is also equivalent to the reflective localization at maps of the form

j_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}\to j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}

for
j\colon U\to X
in
\mathrm{Pro}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{\operatorname{\mathrm{aff}},\fp})
and
w
-contractible.

Proposition 1.2.11
.

Let
f\colon Y\to X
be a map of qcqs schemes. Then

f^{*}\colon\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})\to\mathrm{Sh}(Y_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})

preserves solid sheaves. In fact, if
j\colon U\to X
is affine pro-étale and if
j_{Y}\colon V=U\times_{X}Y\to Y
is its base change along
f
, we have

f^{*}(j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})\simeq(j_{Y})_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}.

As a consequence, if we let
L^{\blacksquare}
be the composition

\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})\xrightarrow{(-)^{\blacksquare}}\mathrm{Sh}^{\blacksquare}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})\xhookrightarrow{i}\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}),

then the natural transformation

L^{\blacksquare}\circ f^{*}\to f^{*}\circ L^{\blacksquare}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

The inclusion
\mathrm{Sh}^{\blacksquare}(Y_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})\subset\mathrm{Sh}(Y_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
preserves colimits by
1.2.8
, and the same is true over
X
; using that
\mathrm{Sh}^{\blacksquare}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
is generated under colimits by the
j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
for
j\colon U\to X
affine pro-étale, to prove that
f^{*}
preserves solid sheaves, it suffices to check that each
f^{*}(j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
is solid. Thus the first part of the lemma follows form the second.

By commutation of the functor
f^{*}
with limits (
1.1.7
), étale base change and
1.2.10
we have
f^{*}(j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})\simeq(j_{Y})_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
: we have proven the second part.

Now, as we have just proven that
f^{*}
preserves solid sheaves, there is a natural transformation
\mathrm{Ex}^{\blacksquare,*}\colon L^{\blacksquare}\circ f^{*}\to f^{*}\circ L^{\blacksquare}
. As noted above, the functor
i
preserves colimits, thus the functor
L^{\blacksquare}
also commutes with colimits. This implies that it suffices to check that
\mathrm{Ex}^{\blacksquare,*}
is an equivalence when evaluated at the
j_{\sharp}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
for
j\colon U\to X
affine pro-étale (
1.2.1
). This then follows from the second part already proven, and the first point of
1.2.8
.
∎

Corollary 1.2.12
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a surjective morphism of qcqs schemes, and let
F
be a pro-étale sheaf of
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-modules on
X
such that
f^{*}F
is solid, then
F
is solid.

Proof.

By
1.1.7
the map
F\to L^{\blacksquare}F
is sent to an isomorphism by
f^{*}
, thus is an equivalence because
f^{*}
is conservative by
1.1.6
.
∎

Remark 1.2.13
.

It is clear from the definition of solid sheaves that for a
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-module, being solid as a
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-module is the same as being solid as a
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}
-module. We can in fact define solid
R
-modules for
R
any quotient of
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}
in the same fashion as above. All of the above results hold in that setting (in particular, they hold for
R=\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
).

The above remark motivates the following definition:

Definition 1.2.14
.

Let
\Lambda
be a solid
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-algebra and let
X
be a scheme. We define the derived category of solid schemes on
X
with
\Lambda
coefficients to be

\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}:=\mathrm{Mod}_{\Lambda}(\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}).

1.3 Derived categories

We keep
X
and
\mathcal{P}
from the above section. Recall that by 5. of
1.2.8
. a complex
K
in
\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
belongs to
\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
if and only if for each
j\colon U\to X
in
\mathrm{Pro}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{aff},\mathrm{fp}})
, the natural map

\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(j_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}},K)\to j_{*}j^{*}K

is an equivalence.

Proposition 1.3.1
.

We have the following:

1.

The inclusion

\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}\subseteq\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})

admits a left adjoint
(-)^{\blacksquare}
.

2.

\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
identifies with the derived category of solid sheaves of
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-modules and
(-)^{\blacksquare}
with the derived functor of the solidification.

3.

The kernel of
K\mapsto K^{\blacksquare}
is a tensor ideal. In particular, there is a unique symmetric monoidal structure
\otimes^{\blacksquare}
on
\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
making the functor
(-)^{\blacksquare}
symmetric monoidal. It is the left derived functor of the induced symmetric monoidal structure the abelian category of solid sheaves. This symmetric monoidal structure commutes with small colimits in each variable and pullbacks.

4.

The above symmetric monoidal structure is closed and its internal
\mathrm{Hom}
are induced by the ones on
\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
.

5.

The t-structure on
\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
is compatible with colimits, left complete and right complete. Hence the inclusion
\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}\subseteq\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
is compatible with colimits.

Proof.

By
1.2.8
5 and
1.2.10
, we may apply
[
CS21
, Lemma 5.9]
to our situation (with compact generators of
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
the
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}[W]
for
W\to X
a pro-étale affine map with
W

w
-contractible) and this proves the first two points. The third point is proven following the same proof as
[
CS21
, Theorem 6.2]
. The fourth point amounts to proving that
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}
sends pairs of
\mathrm{FS}
-solid objects to
\mathrm{FS}
-solid objects; this is true by adjunction.
The compatibility with colimits and right completeness of the last point follow from the second point and the general theory of derived categories from
[
Lura
, Remark C.5.4.11]
, the left completeness follows from the fact that the inclusion into
\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
is t-exact, compatible with limits, and the fact that the latter has a left-complete t-structure by
[
BS15
, Proposition 3.3.3, Proposition 4.2.8 and Proposition 3.2.3]
.

∎

For the rest of this section, we fix a solid
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-algebra
\Lambda
.

Corollary 1.3.2
.

We have the following:

1.

The inclusion

\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\subseteq\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)

admits a left adjoint
(-)^{\blacksquare}
and commutes with colimits.

2.

The formation of
K\mapsto K^{\blacksquare}
, for
K
in
\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)
commutes
with any base change of qcqs schemes.

3.

The kernel of
K\mapsto K^{\blacksquare}
is a tensor ideal. In particular, there is a unique symmetric monoidal structure
\otimes^{\blacksquare}
on
\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
making the functor
(-)^{\blacksquare}
symmetric monoidal. This symmetric monoidal structure commutes with small colimits in each variable and pullbacks, is closed and its internal
\mathrm{Hom}
are induced by the ones on
\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)
.

Proof.

We begin by noting that by
[
Lur22
, Theorem 4.8.4.6]
we have that

\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\simeq\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}\otimes_{\mathrm{D}(*_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}\mathrm{D}(*_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda).

(2)

The same is true for
\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)
. Thus we may use
[
Ram24
, Corollary 1.46]
to conclude that the functor

(-)^{\blacksquare}\otimes\Lambda\colon\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)\to\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}

is a localization functor and that there is a canonical inclusion

\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\subseteq\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda).

The commutation of latter inclusion with colimits follows from the fact in the commutative square of right adjoints

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}}

the vertical functors are conservative and compatible with colimits by
[
Lur22
, Corollary 4.2.3.5 and Corollary 4.2.3.2]
, and the bottom horizontal map is compatible with colimits by
1.3.1
. The second and third points are direct applications of formula
2
.

∎

The following lemma is well-known and can be assembled from pieces in Martini-Wolf’ work on internal category theory.

Lemma 1.3.3
.

Let
(\mathfrak{X},\mathscr{O})
be a ringed
\infty
-topos.
There exists a functor

\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{O}}\colon\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}})

that sends
U\in\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{op}}
to
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{O}_{\mid U}}(\mathrm{Sh}(\mathfrak{X}_{/U},\Sp))
and a map in
\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{op}}
to the restriction functor.

Proof.

First, as a consequence of
[
MW22
, Proposition 2.6.3.7]
, the functor

U\mapsto\mathfrak{X}_{/U}
can be endowed with the structure of the initial presentable category internal to the topos
\mathfrak{X}
, that it denoted by
\Omega^{\otimes}
. The objet
\mathscr{O}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Shv}(\mathfrak{X},\Sp))
defines (see the paragraph just after
[
MW22
, Definition 2.5.2.1]
) a commutative
\mathfrak{X}
-spectrum (
[
MW22
, Definition 2.5.3.13]
, this is a commutative ring objet in the
\mathfrak{X}
-category
\mathrm{Sp}(\Omega)
). In particular, we can take modules internal to
\mathfrak{X}
and by
[
MW22
, Proposition 2.5.3.8]
, the
\mathfrak{X}
-category

\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{O}}(\mathrm{Sp}(\Omega))
is a presentably symmetric monoidal
\mathfrak{X}
-category. Unravelling the definitions, this provides a functor

\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}})

sending
U
to
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{O}_{\mid U}}(\mathrm{Sh}(\mathfrak{X}_{/U},\Sp))
, which is what we wanted.
The preservation of limits can be checked after composition with the forgetful functor to
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}
where we can use
[
Lura
, Remark 2.1.0.5]
.
∎

Lemma 1.3.4
.

Let
f\colon Y\to X
be a map of qcqs schemes.

1.

The restriction of the functor
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)\to\mathrm{D}(Y_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)
to
\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
lands in
\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
. When
f
varies, these functors assemble to give a functor

\mathrm{D}(-,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\colon\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}).

2.

The restriction of the functor
f_{*}\colon\mathrm{D}(Y_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)\to\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)
to
\mathrm{D}(Y,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
lands in
\mathrm{D}(Y,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
. In particular, this shows that right adjoint
f_{*}^{\blacksquare}
to the functor
f^{*}
of 1. is simply
f_{*}
.

3.

If
f
is weakly étale, then there exists a functor
f_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\colon\mathrm{D}(Y,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
, left adjoint to
f^{*}
.

Moreover, if
F
is
f^{*}
or
f_{\sharp}
, and
F^{\blacksquare}
its solid counterpart, the natural transformation
(-)^{\blacksquare}\circ F\to F^{\blacksquare}\circ(-)^{\blacksquare}
is an equivalence.

Proof.

We begin by proving the first point.
That
f^{*}
restrict to a functor between the solid categories is immediate from
1.2.11
in the case
\Lambda=\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
.
For a general
\Lambda
, note that the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(Y,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(Y,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(Y,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(Y,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}}\Lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}}\Lambda}

is vertically right adjointable.
This follows immediately from the formula
\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\simeq\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}\otimes_{\mathrm{D}(*,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}}\mathrm{D}(*,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
and the bifunctoriality of the relative tensor product, since the forgetful functor
\mathrm{D}(Y,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{D}(Y,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
is
{\mathrm{D}(*,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}}
-linear and colimit preserving.
Thus the case for a general
\Lambda
follows since the forgetful functor is conservative and preserve limits.

The highly coherent functor
\mathrm{D}(-,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
is constructed as follows: by
1.3.3
, we have such a functor for
X\mapsto\mathrm{Sh}((\mathrm{Sch}/X)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Sp)
.
Note that for each scheme
X
, the functor
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Sp)\to\mathrm{Sh}((\mathrm{Sch}/X)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Sp)
is fully faithful, compatible with tensor products and pullbacks. Thus by
[
MW22
, Proposition 2.5.1.13 and Proposition 2.5.2.8]
, we have a functor
\mathrm{D}((-)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)
on schemes, with values in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}})
. Now we may apply
[
Lur22
, Proposition 2.2.1.9]
to obtain both the functor
\mathrm{D}(-,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
valued in
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}})
and the compatibility with the solidification functor.

Because the functor
f^{*}
preserves solid sheaves, the existence of
f_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}
for weakly étale maps, together with its compatibility with solidification is formal.
We now prove that
f_{*}
preserves solid objects.
For this note that

We may assume that
\Lambda=\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
because
f_{*}
is induced by the functor
f_{*}
with
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
coefficients.
Let
K\in\mathrm{D}(Y,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
. By using left-completeness (
1.3.1
5.) and the fact that the inclusion functor of solid complexes in all complexes is t-exact and commutes with limits, we may assume that
K
is bounded below. Next, by a spectral sequence argument, we may assume that
K
is concentrated in degree
0
. In that case using that
f_{*}
preserves colimits of uniformly bounded below complexes (
[
HRS23
, Lemma 5.3]
), that the t-structure on solid sheaves is compatible with colimits, and that the abelian category of solid sheaves is closed under colimits in the abelian category of pro-étale sheaves, we may assume that
K
is compact in
\mathrm{Sh}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
. In that case,
K
is a cofiltered limit of torsion étale sheaves thus we may assume that
K
is a constructible torsion étale sheaf. By
[
BS15
, Lemma 5.4.3]
we conclude as then
f_{*}K
has cohomology sheaves being torsion étale sheaves which are solid by
1.2.9
.
∎

Lemma 1.3.5
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a morphism of schemes. Then the functor
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{D}(Y,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
commutes with all limits.

Proof.

By the first point of
1.3.4
we have a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(Y_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(Y,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}

where the upper horizontal functor preserves limits by
1.1.7
and the vertical functors are fully faithful and preserve limits as they are right adjoint functors.
Thus so does the lower horizontal functor.
∎

.

Consider a cartesian square of schemes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-0.93925pt}{-25.09138pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

The canonical map
f^{*}p_{*}\to q_{*}g^{*}
of functors
\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{D}(Y^{\prime},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
is an equivalence if we are in one of the following cases:

1.

The morphism
p
is proper.

2.

The morphism
f
is smooth and all primes in
\mathcal{P}
are invertible on
Y
.

Proof.

Because the forgetful functor
\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
is conservative and commutes with all
f^{*}
and
p_{*}
’s, we may assume that
\Lambda=\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
. In that case, since all of the involved functors commute with limits by
1.3.5
and the t-structure on solid complexes is left complete by the last point of
1.3.1
, we may replace some
K\in\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
by its truncation
\tau_{\leq n}K
to assume that
K
is bounded below.
Since
p_{*},q_{*}
commute with filtered colimits of uniformly bounded below complexes (
[
HRS23
, Lemma 5.3]
), we may reduce the case where
K
is bounded by writing
\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{n}\tau_{\geq n}K\simeq K
.
If
K
is bounded we may do dévissage to assume that
K
is concentrated in degree
0
.
Using commutation with filtered colimits again,
1.2.8
allows us to reduce to the case where
K
is a torsion constructible étale sheaf in which case the claim follows from ordinary proper/smooth base change in étale cohomology which is
[
sga72
, Théorème 5.1]
and
[
Aut18
,
Tag 0EYU
]
.
∎

Remark 1.3.7
.

As a consequence of
1.3.5
, the functor
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{D}(Y,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
has a left adjoint
f_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}
, which we can think of as relative solid
\Lambda
-homology.
Note that this left adjoint also exists for the non-solid categories by
1.1.7
.
The non-solid version however is not that well behaved.
For example, it will not be homotopy variant, even with torsion coefficients prime to the characteristic, see
[
HHL+25
, §7.1]
.
Relative solid
\Lambda
-homology however is
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant by the proof of
3.2.5
.
Furthermore
1.3.6
shows that for smooth
f
, it is compatible with base change.

For later use we also add the following small observation:

Lemma 1.3.8
.

Assume that all the primes in
\mathcal{P}
are invertible on
X
, then the sheaf
\mu_{\infty,\mathcal{P}}\coloneqq\lim_{n\in\Pi\mathcal{P}}\mu_{n}
is invertible in
\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
(see
1.2.6
for the notation
\Pi\mathcal{P}
).

Proof.

Since all primes in
\mathcal{P}
are invertible on
X
, the map of schemes
\lim_{n\in\Pi\mathcal{P}}\mu_{n}\to X
is a pro-étale cover.
Thus we may assume that a primitive
n
-th root of unity exists for every
n
that is a product of primes in
\mathcal{P}
.
Picking a compatible system of such roots then defines an isomorphism
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}\xrightarrow{\cong}\mu_{\infty,\mathcal{P}}
, which shows the claim.
∎

We end this section with a comparison with usual
\ell
-adic sheaves and complexes of torsion étale sheaves.

Proposition 1.3.9
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme and assume that
\Lambda=\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. Then the functor

\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})

lands in
\mathrm{D}(X,\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\blacksquare}
and gives a symmetric monoidal functor

\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}(X,\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\blacksquare}.

Moreover, the latter induces a fully faithful functor

\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\mathrm{t}\wedge}\to\mathrm{D}(X,\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\blacksquare},

where
\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\mathrm{t}\wedge}
is the left completion of
\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})
.

Proof.

To prove the first two parts of the proposition, as being solid can be checked on cohomology objects, and the functor

\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})
is t-exact, it suffices to prove that if
F\in\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})
, then
\nu^{*}(F)
is solid. This is
1.2.9
. The last part of the proposition follows from the fact that the t-structure on
\mathrm{D}(X,\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\blacksquare}
is left-complete, and that
\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})
is fully faithful after left-completion by
[
BS15
, Proposition 5.3.2]
.
∎

Recall the following definition:

Definition 1.3.10

(Hemo-Richarz-Scholbach)
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme. A complex
K\in\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)
is called constructible if there exists a finite stratification of
X
by quasi-compact locally closed subschemes
such that
K
is dualizable over each stratum. Denote by
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X,\Lambda)
the subcategory of such
K
’s.

Recall also that by
[
HRS23
, Theorem 7.7]
, there is a canonical equivalence

\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\simeq\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})

with the classical category of
\ell
-adic constructible complexes and a the same is true with
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
-coefficients.

Proposition 1.3.11
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme. Let
K\in\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
. Then
K
is solid: we have
K\in\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
.

Proof.

If
K
is dualizable, the functor
-\otimes K
commutes with limits, thus using that
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}=\lim_{n}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}/n\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
, we see that
K\simeq\lim_{n}K/n
and we conclude using
[
HRS23
, Proposition 7.1]
that implies that each
K/n
is a bounded complex of torsion étale sheaves.

In the general case,
K
is dualizable over the strata of some stratification of
X
. Taking the disjoint union of all strata yields a surjective map
g\colon Y\to X
such that
g^{*}K
is dualizable, whence solid. We conclude by
1.2.12
.
∎

Corollary 1.3.12
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme. Then every object of
\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})\subset\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
lies in the full subcategory
\mathrm{D}(X,\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})^{\blacksquare}
.

Proof.

Given
K\in\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X,\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
, there exists
L\in\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{cons}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
such that
L\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{l}}\mathbb{Q}_{l}\simeq K
, by
[
HS23
, Corollary 2.4]
. Thus the claim follows from
1.3.11
.
∎

1.4 Solid sheaves through condensed category theory

We now provide an alternative way of passing to subcategories of solid objects using the machinery of condensed
\infty
-categories.
Condensed
\infty
-categories can be viewed as
\infty
-categories internal to the
\infty
-topos
\Cond(\mathrm{Ani})
of condensed anima.
A general theory of
\infty
-categories internal to an arbitrary
\infty
-topos has been developed in
[
Mar21
,
MW24
,
MW22
]
.
We recall some of the basic ideas here.

Definition 1.4.1
.

A
condensed
\infty
-category

\mathcal{C}
is a hypersheaf of
\infty
-categories on the site
\ProFin=*_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
of profinite sets
\mathcal{C}\colon\ProFin\to\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
.
A functor of condensed
\infty
-categories is simply a natural transformation.
Similarly a symmetric monoidal condensed
\infty
-category is simply a hypersheaf on profinite sets with values
\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty})
.

If
\mathcal{C}
is a condensed category and
s\colon S\to T
is a map of profinite sets, we often denote the associated transition functor
\mathcal{C}(T)\to\mathcal{C}(S)
by
s^{*}
.

Here are the main examples that we use in this paper:

Example 1.4.2
.

1.

Unstraightening the codomain fibration and restricting along
\ProFin^{\mathrm{op}}\to\Cond(\mathrm{Ani})^{\mathrm{op}}
gives a functor

\ProFin^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty};\;\;\;S\mapsto\Cond(\mathrm{Ani})_{/S}.

We denote this condensed
\infty
-category by
\underline{\Cond}(\mathrm{Ani})
.
Its role in condensed category theory is analogous to the role of
\infty
-category of
\mathrm{Ani}
in usual
\infty
-category theory.
Taking the cartesian monoidal structure at every
S
,
\underline{\Cond}(\mathrm{Ani})
canonically refines to a symmetric monoidal condensed category, see
[
MW22
, Remark 2.6.2.7]
.

2.

For any presentable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
there is a version of the above example with coefficients in
\mathcal{C}
given by

\ProFin^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty};\;\;\;S\mapsto\Cond(S,\mathcal{C})=\Cond(\mathrm{Ani})_{/S}\otimes\mathcal{C},

where
\otimes
denotes the Lurie tensor product. We denote this condensed
\infty
-category by
\underline{\Cond}(\mathcal{C})
. If furthermore
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}})
, then
\underline{\Cond}(\mathcal{C})
canonically refines to a symmetric monoidal condensed
\infty
-category.

3.

Let
\Lambda
be a condensed
E_{\infty}
-ring, i.e. a commutative algebra in
\Cond(\Sp)
. For any profinite set
S
we still denote by
\Lambda
the base change of
\Lambda
to
\Cond(S,\Sp)
.
Then there is a symmetric monoidal condensed category of
\Lambda
-modules

\underline{\mathrm{Mod}}_{\Lambda}\colon\ProFin^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty});\;\;\;S\mapsto\mathrm{Mod}_{\Lambda}(\Cond(S,\Sp)),

see
1.3.3
.

4.

If
\Lambda
is a solid
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-algebra as before, we get also get a symmetric monoidal condensed category of solid
\Lambda
-modules

\underline{\mathrm{Solid}}_{\Lambda}\colon\ProFin^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty});\;\;\;S\mapsto\mathrm{D}(S,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}.

5.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme, we have a condensed category

\underline{\mathrm{Sh}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})\colon\ProFin^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty});\;\;\;S\mapsto\mathrm{Sh}((X\times S)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}).

As before, we also have a spectral version
\underline{\mathrm{Sh}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathrm{Sp})
and for any condensed ring spectrum
\Lambda
, we have a
\Lambda
-linear version
\underline{\mathrm{D}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)\colon S\mapsto\mathrm{Mod}_{\Lambda}(\mathrm{Sh}((X\times S)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathrm{Sp}))
.

It turns out that all the examples of condensed categories listed above enjoy some special features. That is, they are
presentable
in the following sense.

Definition 1.4.3

(
[
MW22
, §2.4]
)
.

A condensed
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
is called presentable if the following two conditions are satisfied:

1.

The sheaf
\mathcal{C}\colon\ProFin^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
factors through the (non-full) subcategory
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}\subseteq\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
.

2.

For any
s\colon S\to T
, the left adjoint
s_{!}
to the functor
s^{*}\colon\mathcal{C}(T)\to\mathcal{C}(S)
is such that for any other map
\alpha\colon T^{\prime}\to T
the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}(T)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}(S)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}(T^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}(S\times_{T}T^{\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\alpha\times_{T}S)^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s\times_{T}T^{\prime})^{*}}

is horizontally left adjointable.

Remark 1.4.4
.

It is easy to check that all examples appearing in
1.4.2
are indeed presentable.

The
\infty
-category of condensed
\infty
-categories is cartesian closed
[
Mar21
, Proposition 3.2.11]
.
For two condensed
\infty
-categories
\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}
we denote the internal hom by

\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}^{\cond}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})\in\Cond(\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty})

and call it the condensed category of functors from
\mathcal{C}
to
\mathcal{D}
.
Taking global sections we obtain an
\infty
-category

\mathrm{Fun}^{\cond}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})=\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}^{\cond}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})(\ast)

that we call the
\infty
-category of condensed functors from
\mathcal{C}
to
\mathcal{D}
.
In particular
\Cond(\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty})
is naturally enriched over
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
and thus defines an
(\infty,2)
-category (see
[
Mar21
, Remark 3.4.3]
).
We define an
adjunction
between condensed categories to simply be an adjunction in the
(\infty,2)
-category
\Cond(\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty})

[
MW24
, Definition 3.1.1]
.
Adjoint functors between condensed categories also admit the following very explicit description:

Proposition 1.4.5

(
[
Mar21
, Proposition 3.2.9]
)
.

Let
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
be a functor of condensed categories.
Then
f
admits (resp. is) a left adjoint if and only if for any
S\in\ProFin
the functor
f(S)
admits a left (resp. right) adjoint and for any map
s\colon S\to T
the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}(T)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{D}(T)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}(S)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{D}(S)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f(T)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f(S)}

is horizontally left (resp. right) adjointable.

Definition 1.4.6
.

We define the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\cond}
of presentable condensed categories to be the subcategory of
\Cond(\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty})
spanned by the presentable condensed categories and left adjoint functors between them.

Evaluating at the point defines a functor
\Gamma\colon\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\cond}\to\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}
. For later use we recall the following:

Lemma 1.4.7
.

The functor
\Gamma\colon\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\cond}\to\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}
preserves colimits.

Proof.

See the proof of
[
MW22
, Proposition 2.6.3.2]
.
∎

By
[
MW22
, §2.6]
there is a symmetric monoidal structure
-\otimes^{\cond}-
on
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\cond}
that can be viewed as a condensed variant of the Lurie tensor product of presentable
\infty
-categories.

Definition 1.4.8
.

A presentably symmetric monoidal condensed category is a commutative algebra
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\cond})
.

This may sound scary at first sight, but it admits the following more explicit description:

Lemma 1.4.9
.

A symmetric monoidal condensed category
\mathcal{C}
is presentably symmetric monoidal if and only if the following three conditions are satisfied:

1.

The underlying condensed category of
\mathcal{C}
is presentable.

2.

For any
S\in\ProFin
the tensor product
-\otimes-\colon\mathcal{C}(S)\times\mathcal{C}(S)\to\mathcal{C}(S)
preserves colimits in each variable.

3.

For any map
s\colon S\to T
of profinite sets, the left adjoint
s_{!}\colon\mathcal{C}(S)\to\mathcal{C}(T)
of
s^{*}
satisfies the projection formula, that is the canonical map

s_{!}(a\otimes s^{*}b)\to s_{!}(a)\otimes b

is an equivalence for any
a\in\mathcal{C}(S)
and
b\in\mathcal{C}(T)
.

Proof.

This follows from unwinding the definitions and
1.4.5
, see e.g.
[
Cno23
, after Definition 2.15]
.
∎

Remark 1.4.10
.

Using the above Lemma it is easy to check that all examples in
1.4.2
are presentably symmetric monoidal.

Remark 1.4.11
.

Note that the symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
is cartesian closed and for two presentable condensed categories, the internal hom is given by the condensed subcategory

\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}^{\cond,\mathrm{L}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})\subseteq\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})

spanned by the left adjoint functors (this essentially follows from the definition of the
-\otimes^{\cond}-
as the universal bilinear functor, see
[
MW22
, §2.6]
).
For a fixed presentable condensed category
\mathcal{T}
, we thus obtain a map

\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}^{\cond,\mathrm{L}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})\to\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}^{\cond,\mathrm{L}}(\mathcal{C}\otimes^{\cond}\mathcal{T},\mathcal{D}\otimes^{\cond}\mathcal{T})

that is natural in
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
as the transpose of the natural map

\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}^{\cond,\mathrm{L}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})\otimes^{\cond}\mathcal{C}\otimes^{\cond}\mathcal{T}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{ev}\otimes\mathrm{Id}}\mathcal{D}\otimes^{\cond}\mathcal{T}.

Evaluating at the point we thus obtain a natural map of
\infty
-categories

\mathrm{Fun}^{\cond,\mathrm{L}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})\to\mathrm{Fun}^{\cond,\mathrm{L}}(\mathcal{C}\otimes^{\cond}\mathcal{T},\mathcal{D}\otimes^{\cond}\mathcal{T})

and one furthermore checks that after passing to maximal subgroupoids, this functor recovers the action of the functor
-\otimes^{\cond}\mathcal{T}\colon\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\cond}\to\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\cond}
on mapping spaces.

In more coherent fashion, one can show that
-\otimes^{\cond}-
upgrades to an
(\infty,2)
-functor. Since we do not actually need the full coherence in this paper we do not spell this out here.

Lemma 1.4.12
.

Let
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
be a map in
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\cond}
that admits a further left adjoint
g\colon\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{C}
.
Then for any
\mathcal{T}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\cond}
the functor
g\otimes^{\cond}\mathcal{T}
is left adjoint to
f\otimes^{\cond}\mathcal{T}
.

Proof.

This is immediate from
1.4.11
.
∎

Definition 1.4.13
.

We call a functor
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
of condensed categories fully faithful if for every
S\in\ProFin
the functor
f(S)
is fully faithful.

Lemma 1.4.14
.

Let
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
be a map in
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\cond}
.
Then for any
\mathcal{T}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\cond}
, if the right adjoint
g
of
f
is fully faithful (i.e.
f
is a
condensed localization
), then so is the right adjoint
g\otimes^{\cond}\mathcal{T}
of
f\otimes^{\cond}\mathcal{T}
.

Proof.

From
[
MW22
, Proposition 2.6.2.11]
, we have identifications:

\mathcal{C}\otimes^{\cond}\mathcal{T}\simeq\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}^{\cond,\mathrm{R}}(\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{C})\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274pt\mathcal{D}\otimes^{\cond}\mathcal{T}\simeq\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}^{\cond,\mathrm{R}}(\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathcal{D})

where
\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}^{\cond,\mathrm{R}}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
is the condensed subcategory of
\underline{\mathrm{Fun}}^{\cond}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
spanned by condensed right adjoints for
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
two condensed
\infty
-categories.
The condensed functor
g\otimes^{\cond}\mathcal{T}
identifies with composition with
g
through the above equivalences. The result then follows from
[
Mar21
, Proposition 3.8.4]
.
∎

Lemma 1.4.15
.

Let
f\colon Y\to X
be a map of schemes. Then the functor
f_{\sharp}\colon\mathrm{Sh}(Y_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})\to\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})
naturally enhances to a condensed left adjoint functor

f_{\sharp}\colon\underline{\mathrm{Sh}}(Y_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})\to\underline{\mathrm{Sh}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})
.

Proof.

To see that we have a condensed functor, we need to prove that if
g\colon K\to S
is a map of profinite sets, the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}((Y\times K)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}((X\times K)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}((Y\times S)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}((X\times S)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}^{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{*}_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{X}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}^{S}}

commutes. Passing to right adjoints and noting that
g_{X}
is integral, this follows from integral base change (
[
HHL+25
, Proposition 6.18]
). As the above square is horizontally right adjointable,
f_{\sharp}
is indeed a condensed left adjoint. ∎

From this point on, we let
\Lambda
be a solid
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-algebra where
\mathcal{P}
is as before.

Definition 1.4.16
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\underline{\mathrm{Mod}}_{\Lambda}}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathrm{cond}})
, the
solidification
of
\mathcal{C}
is the tensor product

\mathcal{C}^{\blacksquare}\coloneqq\mathcal{C}\otimes^{\mathrm{cond}}_{\underline{\mathrm{Mod}}_{\Lambda}}\underline{\mathrm{Solid}}_{\Lambda}.

We now have a second way of defining a category of solid pro-étale sheaves, that is we may consider the underlying category of the solidification
\underline{\mathrm{D}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
of
\underline{\mathrm{D}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)
in the above sense.
The next proposition shows that this agrees with the category of
1.2.14
.

Proposition 1.4.17
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme. Then
1.4.16
does not create a clash of notations:
the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
of
1.2.14
is naturally equivalent to
\underline{\mathrm{D}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}(*)
.

Proof.

We may assume that
\Lambda=\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
.
Note that both categories in discussion are reflective subcategories of
\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
.
For the second one this follows using
1.4.14
with the observation that
\underline{\mathrm{D}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\simeq\underline{\mathrm{Sh}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})\otimes^{\cond}\underline{\mathrm{Solid}}_{\Lambda}
.
We have two solidification functors

(-)^{\blacksquare}\colon\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})\to\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}

and

(-)^{\blacksquare^{\prime}}\colon\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})\to\underline{\mathrm{D}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}(\ast).

Our goal is to show that these two reflective subcategories agrees.
At first suppose that we are given a map of schemes
f\colon Y\to X
. We claim that the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{D}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{D}}(Y_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{D}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{D}}(Y_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\blacksquare^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\blacksquare^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}

(the fact that it commutes is just bifunctoriality of
-\otimes^{\cond}-
) is vertically right adjointable.
This follows because the square that we get after passing to right adjoints vertically is the square that we get from passing to right adjoints (horizontally and vertically) in

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{D}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{D}}(Y_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{D}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathrm{D}}(Y_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\blacksquare^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\blacksquare^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{\sharp}\otimes^{\cond}\underline{\mathrm{Solid}}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}}}

where the bottom line is obtained by tensoring the condensed functor from
1.4.15
with
\underline{\mathrm{Solid}}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}}
.
Since this square commutes by bifunctoriality of
-\otimes^{\cond}-
we have shown the claim.

Now pick a surjective morphism
f\colon E\to X
of schemes where
E
is
0
-dimensional and all local rings are separably closed fields using
1.1.4
.
A complex
K
in
\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}})
is contained in
\underline{\mathrm{D}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}})^{\blacksquare}(\ast)
if and only if
f^{*}K
is contained in
\underline{\mathrm{D}}(E_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}})^{\blacksquare}(\ast)
: indeed
f^{*}
is conservative by
1.1.6
and applying
f^{*}
to the canonical map
K\to K^{\blacksquare^{\prime}}
gives the canonical map
f^{*}K\to(f^{*}K)^{\blacksquare^{\prime}}
, by the adjointability that we have just shown.
Since the same is true in
\mathrm{D}(X,{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}})^{\blacksquare}
by
1.2.12
, we have reduced the Proposition to
X=E
. But in this case

\mathrm{D}(E_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}})^{\blacksquare}(\ast)=\underline{\mathrm{Solid}}_{{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}}}(\pi_{0}(E))=\mathrm{D}(E,{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}})^{\blacksquare}

as full subcategories of
\mathrm{D}(E_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}})
.
Here the first equality follows from
[
MW22
, Proposition 2.6.2.11]
,
[
MW24
, Theorem 7.1.1]
and
1.1.4
.
The second one is true by definition of
\underline{\mathrm{Solid}}_{{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}}}(\pi_{0}(E))
.
This completes the proof.
∎

2 Pro-étale motives.

In this section, we construct the category of pro-étale motivic spectra over a base scheme and we prove that they form a coefficient system, affording a full six functors formalism
3
3

3

The only caveat is that the exceptional functors will only be defined for finitely presented maps and not for arbitrary maps of finite type which is the usual assumption.
. Our construction closely follows classical material from
[
MV99
,
Ayo07a
,
Ayo07b
,
CD19
,
Kha16
,
Hoy17
]
.
We begin in
Section

2.1
by defining the desired category of pro-étale motivic spectra based on the notion of a
weakly smooth
scheme,
2.1.6
.
In
Section

2.2
we study the basic functoriality properties of this construction and especially investigate the push-forward along a closed immersion
2.2.4
.
This is one of the ingredients for the proof of the
localization theorem
in this setting, which we prove in
Section

2.3
.
Given the localization theorem, we can follow the general construction principles of motivic six functor formalisms.
The only difference is that in our setting is the localization theorem only holds for quasi-compact open immersions.
In
Section

2.4
and
Section

2.5
we verify that the key steps in the construction of a motivic six functor formalism still go through in that case, if one only aims to to construct the exceptional operations for finitely presented morphisms.
Finally, in
Section

2.6
we show that under suitable cohomological finiteness assumptions, étale motives embed into pro-étale motives.

2.1 Basic definitions and properties

Definition 2.1.1
.

Let
X
be a scheme.
We define the
large pro-étale site of
S
-schemes

\mathrm{Sch}_{X}^{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
to be the category of all schemes over
X
, equipped with the Grothendieck-topology generated by collections of weakly étale morphisms that are coverings in the fpqc topology.
In particular we may consider the
large pro-étale
\infty
-topos
of hypercomplete sheaves of anima on this site.
We denote this
\infty
-category by
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X).

Definition 2.1.2
.

Let
X
be a scheme.

1.

We define
L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
to be the reflective subcategory of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
spanned by
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant sheaves. We denote by
L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
the localization functor.

2.

We define
L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(X)
to be the category of pointed objects in
L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
.

3.

We define
L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{S}^{1}}(X)
to be the stabilization of
L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(X)
. There is an infinite suspension functor

\Sigma^{\infty}\colon L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(X)\to L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{S}^{1}}(X).

By composing left adjoints we get a
motivic localization functor

L_{\mot}\colon\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{X})\rightarrow L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)

which can be described more explicitly following the ideas of Morel and Voevodsky.
To that end, recall the algebraic
n
-simplex

\Delta_{X}^{n}\coloneqq\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}[t_{0},\dots,t_{n}]/(\sum_{i=0}^{n}t_{i}-1))\times_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})}X

over
X
. When
n
varies, these assemble to a simplicial
X
-scheme

\Delta^{\bullet}_{X}\colon\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sch}_{X}.

For any presheaf
F
on
\mathrm{Sch}_{X}
we define

\Sing(F)=\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{n\in\Delta}F(-\times\Delta^{n}_{X}).

This assembles to a functor
\Sing\colon\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{X})\rightarrow\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{X})
.
We write
\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{X})
for the full subcategory spanned by the
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant presheaves.
Then the same arguments as in
[
AE17
, Theorem 4.25]
show:

Lemma 2.1.3
.

Let
X
be a scheme. The functor
\Sing(-)
lands in the full subcategory
\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{X})
and defines a left adjoint to the inclusion functor.

Proposition 2.1.4
.

Let
X
be a scheme. The motivic localization functor
L_{\mot}\colon\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{X})\rightarrow L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
commutes with finite products.

Proof.

We pick a regular cardinal
\kappa
such that the inclusion
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)\hookrightarrow\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{X})
commutes with
\kappa
-filtered colimits.
Note that we have an endofunctor

\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{X})\xrightarrow{L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\circ L_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{X})

together with a canonical natural transformation
\mathrm{Id}\rightarrow L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\circ L_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
.
We may therefore consider the endofunctor
L_{M}=\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{\tau<\kappa}(L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\circ L_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})^{\tau}
.
Then for any presheaf
F
, the presheaf
L_{M}(F)
is
\kappa
-filtered colimit of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-local presheaves and therefore
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-local.
Furthermore the usual cofinality arguments (see the proof of
[
Lur09
, Lemma 6.3.3.4]
) show that we also have an equivalence

L_{M}\simeq\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{\tau<\kappa}(L_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\circ L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}})^{\tau}

and thus
L_{M}(F)
is also a pro-étale sheaf for any presheaf
F
.
Thus
L_{M}
defines a functor

\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{X})\rightarrow L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)

and it is easy to see that it is a left adjoint to the inclusion and therefore equivalent to
L_{\mot}
.
This explicit description proves the claim since sifted colimits commute with finite products.
∎

We fix notations for later use:

1.

The cartesian product defines a symmetric monoidal structure on
L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
and by Proposition
2.1.4
, the motivic localization functor is symmetric monoidal.
Moreover, the Yoneda embedding, composed with the motivic localization functor, induces a functor

\mathrm{Sch}_{X}\to L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X).

If
Y\in\mathrm{Sch}_{X}
, we also denote by
Y
its image in
L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
.

2.

There is also a functor

(-)_{+}\colon L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)\to L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(X)

that freely adds a base point; by
[
Rob15
, Corollary 5.4]
, there is a unique symmetric monoidal structure on
L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(X)
such that
(-)_{+}
enhances to a symmetric monoidal functor that is the universal functor from
L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
to a pointed category. Hence, we obtain a symmetric monoidal functor

\mathrm{Sch}_{X}\to L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(X)

sending a scheme
X
to
X_{+}
. Note that by
[
Rob15
, Proposition 4.10]
, the functor
\Sigma^{\infty}
also naturally enhances to a symmetric monoidal functor which is universal among the functors from
L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(X)
to a stable category.

3.

We also have a symmetric monoidal functor

\mathrm{Sch}_{X,\bullet}\to L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(X)

that sends a pointed
X
-scheme
(Y,s)
, where
s\colon X\to Y
is a section of
Y\to X
, to an object that we also denote by
(Y,s)
.

Definition 2.1.5
.

Let
X
be a scheme and let
p,q\in\mathbb{N}
be nonnegative integers with
p\geqslant q
. The
motivic sphere

\mathrm{S}^{p,q}\in L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(X)
is the
objet

\mathrm{S}^{p,q}\coloneqq\Sigma^{p-q}({\mathbb{G}_{m}},1)^{\otimes q}

obtained by suspending
p-q
times the
X
-scheme
{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\times X
pointed at
1
. If
u\geqslant v
we have
\mathrm{S}^{p,q}\otimes\mathrm{S}^{u,v}\simeq\mathrm{S}^{p+u,q+v}
. Note that as in
[
MV99
, Lemma 2.15 and Corollary 2.18]
we have that
\mathrm{S}^{2,1}\simeq(\mathbb{P}^{1}_{X},*)
is the projective space pointed at any point.

We define the category
L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathbb{P}^{1}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
, to be the stable presentably monoidal category obtained by
\otimes
-inverting the sphere
\mathrm{S}^{2,1}
in the sense of
[
Rob15
, Definition 4.8]
. As
\mathrm{S}^{1,0}\simeq\mathrm{S}^{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(X)}
is inverted, we obtain a stable
\infty
-category. We denote by
\mathbb{S}(q)[p]
the image of
\mathrm{S}^{p,q}
in this category.
We write

\mathcal{F}(n)\simeq\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathbb{S}(1)^{n}

for any
\mathcal{F}\in L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathbb{P}^{1}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. Finally we write

\mathbb{S}[-]\colon\mathrm{Sch}_{X}\to L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(X)\to L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathbb{P}^{1}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)

the obvious functor.

We now define the analogue in our pro-étale setting, of the smooth-étale site.

Definition 2.1.6
.

We say that a map
f\colon Y\to X
of schemes is weakly smooth if it can be written as a finite composition of weakly étale maps and smooth maps.

Lemma 2.1.7
.

Let
f:Y\to X
be a weakly smooth map. Then Zariski-locally on
Y
we can write
f
as a composition

Y\to\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X}\to X

with
Y\to\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X}
weakly étale and
\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X}\to X
the canonical projection.

Proof.

Assume that
f
is a composition

Y=X_{N+1}\xrightarrow{f_{N}}X_{N}\to\cdots\to X_{1}\xrightarrow{f_{0}}X_{0}=X

with each
f_{i}
either weakly étale or smooth. We prove the proposition by induction on
N
.
If
N=0
, we are looking at a single morphism which is either weakly étale, in which case we are done, or smooth in which case it follows from the structure theorem for smooth morphisms (
[
Aut18
,
Theorem 039Q
]
).

Let
N\geqslant 1
and assume that any morphism
f
that can be written as above with
N-1
maps is Zariski-locally on the source in the form stated in the lemma. Let
f\colon Y\to X
that can be written as the composition of
N
maps as above. We can thus write
f=f_{0}\circ g
with
g\colon Y\to Z
a composition of at most
N-1
maps as above.
By our induction hypothesis, locally on
Y
the map
g
is of the form
Y\to\mathbb{A}^{n}_{Z}\to Z
. Up to replacing
Y
by one of its Zariski opens we can therefore assume that we are in a situation

Y\to\mathbb{A}^{n}_{Z}\to Z\xrightarrow{f_{0}}X

with
Y\to\mathbb{A}^{n}_{Z}
weakly étale. There are two cases. If
f_{0}
is weakly étale, then have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}

with
u
weakly étale as the composition of a weakly étale map and the product with
\mathbb{A}^{n}
of a weakly étale map and we are done.
If
f_{0}
is smooth, then locally on
Z
it is of the form
Z\to\mathbb{A}^{m}_{X}\to X
with
Z\to\mathbb{A}^{m}_{X}
étale. Let
V
be an open subset of
Z
on which
f_{0}
has this property and let
U
be its inverse image on
Y
. We have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{V}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbb{A}^{m}_{X}}\simeq\mathbb{A}^{n+m}_{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{A}^{m}_{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{w-\acute{e}tale}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\acute{e}tale}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{\acute{e}tale}}

which proves that we can write
U\to X
as

U\to\mathbb{A}^{n+m}_{X}\to X

with
U\to\mathbb{A}^{n+m}_{X}
weakly étale. This finishes the proof.
∎

Definition 2.1.8
.

Let
X
be a scheme.

1.

We define the
pro-étale unstable (resp. unstable pointed) motivic homotopy category

\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
(resp.
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(X)
)
of
X
to be the smallest full subcategory of
L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
(resp.
L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(X)
) that is closed under small colimits and contains all objects of the form
U
(resp.
U_{+}
) for any weakly smooth
X
-scheme
U
.

2.

The
\mathrm{S}^{1}
-stable pro-étale motivic homotopy category
of
X
is the smallest stable full subcategory
\SH^{\mathrm{S}^{1}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
of
L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathrm{S}^{1}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
that is closed under small colimits and contains all objects of the form
\Sigma^{\infty}U_{+}
for any weakly smooth
X
-scheme
U
.

3.

The
stable pro-étale motivic homotopy category
of
X
is the smallest stable full subcategory
\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
of
L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathbb{P}^{1}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
that is closed under small colimits and contains all objects of the form
\mathbb{S}[U](n)
for any weakly smooth
X
-scheme
U
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Remark 2.1.9
.

Let
X
be a scheme.
It is easy to see that
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(X)
is canonically equivalent to the category of pointed objects in
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
.
In the same way, the category
\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
is given by the
(\mathbb{P}^{1},\infty)
-stabilization of
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(X)
. We denote by

\Sigma^{\infty}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\colon\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(X)\to\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)

the induced functor.

Remark 2.1.10
.

Let
X
be a scheme. The pro-étale topology induces a topology on
\mathrm{WSm}_{X}
. The map
\mathrm{WSm}_{X}\to\mathrm{Sch}_{X}
therefore induces a map

L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X})\to L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)

from the category of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant pro-étale sheaves on
\mathrm{WSm}_{X}
. The same proof as
[
Kha24
, Proposition 2]
(replacing the Nisnevich topology with the pro-étale topology) shows that this functor is fully faithful and that its essential image is
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
. As in
[
Kha24
, Proposition 4]
,
\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
is then equivalent to the
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-stabilization of the subcategory of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant objects of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X})
.

Note that since the tensor products on the categories defined in
2.1.2
commute with colimits in both variables, we get an induced symmetric monoidal structure on the categories defined in
2.1.8
.

2.2 Functoriality

Let us now discuss the basic functoriality of all these constructions:

2.2.1
Basic Functoriality.

Let
f\colon X\rightarrow Y
be any morphism of schemes.
There is an induced morphism
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(Y)\rightarrow\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
of
\infty
-topoi.

These functor give rise to an adjunction

f^{*}\colon{\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(Y)}\leftrightarrows{\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)}\colon{f_{*}}

where the left adjoint is canonically symmetric monoidal and this is also true for all other of the above categories.

2.2.2
Addtional functorialty.

For any morphism of schemes
f\colon X\rightarrow Y
, the forgetful functor

\mathrm{Sch}_{X}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sch}_{Y}

gives rise to a further left adjoint

f_{\sharp}\colon\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)\rightarrow\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(Y)

to
f^{*}
.
The functor
f_{\sharp}
descends to a functor

f_{\sharp}\colon L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathbb{P}^{1}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)\rightarrow L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathbb{P}^{1}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(Y)

that is left adjoint to
f^{*}
.
Furthermore for any
\mathcal{F}\in{L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathbb{P}^{1}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)}
and
\mathcal{G}\in{L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathbb{P}^{1}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(Y)}
the unit map
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow f^{*}f_{\sharp}\mathcal{F}
induces a morphism

f_{\sharp}(\mathcal{F}\otimes f^{*}\mathcal{G})\rightarrow f_{\sharp}(\mathcal{F})\otimes\mathcal{G.}

This map is an equivalence because it reduces to representables where it is clear.
This shows that the functor
f_{\sharp}
is in fact a morphism of
L_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\mathrm{Sh}^{\mathbb{P}^{1}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(Y)
-modules.
Furthermore we note that for
any
cartesian square of schemes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

the canonical morphism
p_{\sharp}q^{*}\rightarrow g^{*}f_{\sharp}
is an isomorphism.
One can again easily check this on representables.
All of this discussion is also valid for the unstable and pointed unstable categories.

2.2.3
Additional smooth functoriality.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be weakly smooth.
Then the functor
f^{*}\colon\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(Y)\rightarrow\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
admits a left adjoint
f_{\sharp}
such that

1.

The weakly smooth projection formula is satisfied, i.e. the canonical map

f_{\sharp}(\mathcal{F}\otimes f^{*}\mathcal{G})\rightarrow(f_{\sharp}\mathcal{F})\otimes\mathcal{G}

is an equivalence for any
\mathcal{F}\in\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
and
\mathcal{G}\in\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(Y)
.

2.

For any cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T\times_{Y}U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}

of schemes, the canonical natural transformation
p_{\sharp}q^{*}\rightarrow g^{*}f_{\sharp}
is an equivalence.

Indeed since weakly smooth maps are stable under composition
f_{\sharp}
is the restriction of the functor constructed above.
This also works for the unstable and pointed unstable versions.

We now show that there is some additional functoriality in the case of a closed immersion.
This is a first step towards the localization theorem in our context.
The first goal of this section is to show the following proposition.

Proposition 2.2.4
.

Let
i\colon Z\rightarrow X
be a closed immersion.
Then the induced functor

i_{*}\colon\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(Z)\rightarrow\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)

preserves weakly contractible colimits (i.e. colimits indexed by weakly contractible simplicial sets) and the induced functor

i_{*}\colon\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(Z)\rightarrow\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(X)

preserves all small colimits.

2.2.5
.

For this we recall some terminology first.
If
\mathcal{C}
is any category with an initial object
\emptyset
, we denote by
\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}_{\emptyset}(\mathcal{C})
the reflective subcategory of
\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}(\mathcal{C})
spanned by those presheaves
\mathcal{F}
such that
\mathcal{F}(\emptyset)\simeq*
.

If
X
is a scheme we call a morphism
\varphi
in
\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{X})
a
motivic equivalence
if
L_{\mot}(\varphi)
is an equivalence.
Analogously we call a morphism in
\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}_{\emptyset}(\mathrm{Sch}_{X})
a motivic equivalence if it is a motivic equivalence when seen as a map in
\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{X})
.

Lemma 2.2.6
.

The functor
i_{*}\colon\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}_{\emptyset}(\mathrm{Sch}_{Z})\rightarrow\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}_{\emptyset}(\mathrm{Sch}_{X})
preserves motivic equivalences.

Proof.

As in
[
Ayo07a
, Théorème 4.5.35]
the functor
i_{*}
preserves
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-local equivalences, so it suffices to see that it preserves pro-étale local equivalences.
Let
f
be a pro-étale local equivalence in
\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}_{\emptyset}(\mathrm{Sch}_{Z})
, i.e. a morphism that becomes an equivalence after pro-étale hypersheafification.
Note that the inclusion
\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}_{\emptyset}(\mathrm{Sch}_{Z})\to\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{Z})
commutes with
\tau_{\leq n}
, i.e. truncations in
\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}_{\emptyset}(\mathrm{Sch}_{Z})
are computed pointwise.
It follows that
i_{*}\colon\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}_{\emptyset}(\mathrm{Sch}_{Z})\rightarrow\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}_{\emptyset}(\mathrm{Sch}_{X})
also commutes with truncations.
To see that
i_{*}f
is a pro-étale local equivalence it suffices, that
\tau_{\leq n}i_{*}f
becomes an equivalence after pro-étale sheafification.
By the preceding discussion, we may thus assume that
f
is a map of
n
-truncated presheaves.
In this case, there is no difference between pro-étale sheafification and hypersheafification, thus it suffices to see that
i_{*}
preserves maps that become equivalences after pro-étale sheafification.

For this the usual argument applies. More precisely, the claim follows as in
[
Ayo07a
, Lemme 4.5.33]
if for any
T\in\mathrm{Sch}_{X}
and any pro-étale covering
\mathcal{U}=\{U_{i}\to T_{Z}\}
of the base change
T_{Z}
of
T
to
Z
there is a covering
\mathcal{V}=\{V_{j}\to T\}
such that the pullback of
\mathcal{V}
to
Z
refines
\mathcal{U}
.
For this we may assume that
T
is affine and all
U_{i}
are pro-étale affines over
T_{Z}
by
[
BS15
, Theorem 2.3.4]
.
Then we may simply take the covering
\{\tilde{U_{i}}\to T,T_{X\setminus Z}\to T\}
where we use the notations of
1.1.1
.
∎

Proof of
2.2.4
.

The first statement is a formal consequence of the above Lemma, see
[
Kha16
, § 7.2]
. The second statement is now formal, see
[
Hoy17
, Proposition 5.1]
for more details.
∎

We also need the following lemma.

Lemma 2.2.7
.

Let
i\colon Z\to X
be a closed immersion.
Then the image of

i^{*}\colon\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)\to\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(Z)

generates
\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(Z)
under colimits.
The analogous claim holds for
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
and
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}
.

Proof.

We can assume
X
to be affine. Using
2.1.7
, and the fact that
i^{*}
commutes with
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-shifts it suffices to show that the
\mathbb{S}[Y]
belong to its image, where
f\colon Y\to Z
factors as

Y\xrightarrow{g}\mathbb{A}^{n}_{Z}\to Z

with
g
pro-étale and
Y
affine.
But the henselization of
g
along the closed immersion
\mathbb{A}^{n}_{Z}\to\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X}
restricts to
Y
over
Z
and the claim follows.
The proofs for
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
and
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}
are the same.
∎

2.3 Localization

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme. The goal of this subsection is to prove the following theorem in our setting:

Theorem 2.3.1

(Localization)
.

Let
i\colon Z\rightarrow X
be a closed immersion and let
j\colon U\rightarrow X
be the complementary open immersion. Assume that
U
is quasi-compact and quasi-separated.
For any
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
the canonical square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\sharp}j^{*}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{*}i^{*}\mathcal{F}}

is cocartesian.

The upshot of our proof is that the localization theorem for the small pro-étale site allows us to reduce to the original proof due to Morel and Voevodsky
[
MV99
, §3 Theorem 2.21]
.

Remark 2.3.2
.

As we assume that the scheme
X
is qcqs, by
[
Aut18
,
Lemma 01PH
and
Lemma 01TV
]
the open subset
U
is qcqs if and only if there is a closed subscheme
Z^{\prime}
of
X
with same underlying topological subset as
Z
, such that
Z^{\prime}\to X
is finitely presented.

Remark 2.3.3
.

The assumptions that the open
U
is quasi-compact is necessary for
2.3.1
to hold.
In fact for non-quasi-compact opens the localization theorem already fails on the level of sheaves on the small pro-étale site, see
2.3.5
below.

We begin with a similar statement for the small pro-étale site.

Proposition 2.3.4
.

The functors
i^{*}\colon\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sh}(Z_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})
and
j^{*}\colon\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sh}(U_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})
are jointly conservative.

Proof.

Using
1.1.4
and
1.1.6
, we may reduce to the case where
X
is reduced,
0
-dimensional, affine and all of its local rings are separably closed fields.
In this case,
i^{*}
and
j^{*}
identify with the pullback functors
\Cond(\mathrm{Ani})_{/\pi_{0}(X)}\to\Cond(\mathrm{Ani})_{/\pi_{0}(Z)}
and
\Cond(\mathrm{Ani})_{/\pi_{0}(X)}\to\Cond(\mathrm{Ani})_{/\pi_{0}(U)}
.
Since a quasi-compact open of a profinite set is clopen, we have an isomorphism of profinite sets
\pi_{0}(X)\simeq\pi_{0}(Z)\amalg\pi_{0}(U)
.
Thus the two above functors are clearly jointly conservative, as desired.
∎

Remark 2.3.5
.

The conclusion of
2.3.4
does not hold if the open subset
U
is not quasi-compact.
Indeed, consider the profinite set
\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
and the open closed decomposition
\mathbb{N}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}\hookleftarrow\{\infty\}
.
Then in
\Cond(\mathrm{Ani})_{/\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}}
we have a canonical map

\mathbb{N}\amalg\{\infty\}\to\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}.

This map is clearly not an isomorphism (the domain is equivalent to
\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
equipped with the discrete topology).
However it also clearly becomes an isomorphism after restricting to
\mathbb{N}
and
\{\infty\}
.

2.3.6
.

Observe that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}_{\emptyset}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}^{\operatorname{\mathrm{aff}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}_{\emptyset}(Z_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}^{\operatorname{\mathrm{aff}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}_{\emptyset}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}^{\operatorname{\mathrm{aff}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}(Z_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{*}}

commutes: the left-hand square commutes by definition; in the right-hand square, all functors commute with weakly contractible colimits and
\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}_{\emptyset}(Z_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}^{\operatorname{\mathrm{aff}}})
is generated by representables under weakly contractible colimits; as the case of representables follows from the fact that the pro-étale topology is sub-canonical
[
Aut18
,
Tag 098Z
]
, the right-hand square indeed commutes.

We now use notations introduced in
1.1.1
. We have a left adjoint
\widetilde{(-)}
to the pullback
X^{\operatorname{\mathrm{aff}}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\to Z^{\operatorname{\mathrm{aff}}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
, so that at the level of presheaves,
i^{*}
is given by

P\mapsto(U\mapsto P(\widetilde{U})).

Since
\tilde{\emptyset}=\emptyset
, this preserves presheaves that send
\emptyset
to
*
. Let
\mathcal{F}
be in
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})
; using the above commutative diagram, the sheaf
i_{*}i^{*}\mathcal{F}
is therefore given by the sheafification of the assertion

V\mapsto\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{\left.{V}\right|_{Z}}).

Armed with this we can now roughly proceed as in the proof of the localization theorem for ordinary motivic spaces. If
Y\in\mathrm{Sch}_{X}
is a
X
-scheme, we also denote by
Y
its image in
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
.

Definition 2.3.7
.

For any scheme
Y
over
X
and any section
t\colon Z\rightarrow Y_{Z}=Y\times_{X}Z
we define a presheaf of sets
\Phi_{X}(Y,t)
on the category of schemes over
X
by setting

\Phi_{X}(Y,t)(Y^{\prime})=\begin{cases}\mathrm{Hom}_{X}(Y^{\prime},Y)\times_{\mathrm{Hom}_{Z}(Y^{\prime}_{Z},Y_{Z})}*&\text{if }Y_{Z}\neq\emptyset\\
*&\text{else}\end{cases}

where the map
*\to\mathrm{Hom}_{Z}(Y^{\prime}_{Z},Y_{Z})
is given by the map
Y^{\prime}_{Z}\to Z\xrightarrow{t}Y_{Z}
. In other words,

\Phi_{X}(Y,t)=(Y\bigsqcup_{Y_{U}}U)\times_{i_{*}Y_{Z}}X

with the map
X\to i_{*}Y_{Z}
given by
t
and adjunction.

Lemma 2.3.8
.

Let
f\colon V\rightarrow Y
be a pro-étale morphism of affine schemes and let
t^{\prime}\colon Z\rightarrow V_{Z}
and
t\colon Z\rightarrow Y_{Z}
be sections over
Z
such that
f_{Z}\circ t^{\prime}=t
.
Then the induced map

\psi\colon\Phi_{X}(V,t^{\prime})\rightarrow\Phi_{X}(Y,t)

becomes an isomorphism after applying the sheafification functor

L_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\colon\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{X})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X).

Proof.

Let
T
be an affine scheme over
X
. As pro-étale sheafification commutes with restriction to small sites by
[
CM21
, Proposition 7.1]
, it suffices to prove that
L_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\psi_{\mid T^{\mathrm{aff}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
