is an equivalence. By
2.3.4
it suffices to prove that
L_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\psi_{\mid(T_{U})^{\mathrm{aff}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
and
L_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\psi_{\mid(T_{Z})^{\mathrm{aff}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
are equivalences.
As for any
Y\in(T_{U})^{\mathrm{aff}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
, we have
Y_{Z}=\emptyset
, it is clear that
\psi_{\mid(T_{U})^{\mathrm{aff}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
is an equivalence. We remark that if
i_{T}\colon T_{Z}\to T
is the closed immersion obtained by pullback of
i
to
T
, the functor
(i_{T})_{*}
is conservative because it is fully faithful as its left adjoint
i_{T}^{*}
has a further left adjoint given by henselization (see
1.1.1
) and the latter is fully faithful.
Thus it suffices to prove that

(i_{T})_{*}L_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\psi_{\mid(T_{Z})_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}=(i_{T})_{*}(i_{T})^{*}\psi_{\mid T_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}

is an equivalence.
By
2.3.6
, we have that the map of sheaves
(i_{T})_{*}(i_{T})^{*}\psi_{\mid T_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}}
is obtained as the sheafification of

W\in T_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{aff}}\mapsto\psi(\widetilde{W_{\mid T_{Z}}})

so that in particular, it suffices to show (up to replacing
T
by an affine pro-étale scheme over
T
) that
\psi(\widetilde{T_{Z}})
is an equivalence.

To see this we check that the fiber of
\psi(\widetilde{T_{Z}})
over any morphism
q\colon\widetilde{T_{Z}}\rightarrow Y
over
X
that gives rise to an element in
\Phi_{X}(Y,t)(\widetilde{T_{Z}})
is a point.
The latter fiber is isomorphic to

\Phi_{\widetilde{T_{Z}}}(V\times_{Y}\widetilde{T_{Z}},t\times_{Z}T_{Z})(\widetilde{T_{Z}})

so it suffices to see that this set is a point.
But an element in there is given by a map of schemes
s\colon\widetilde{T_{Z}}\rightarrow\widetilde{T_{Z}}\times_{X}V
that makes the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T_{Z}\times_{X}V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T_{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T_{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{T_{Z}}\times_{X}V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{T_{Z}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{T_{Z}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t\times_{Z}T_{Z}}

commute.
It follows that there is a unique such map
s
by the universal property of the henselization.
This proves the claim.
∎

Now we are able to prove the localization theorem in the pro-étale setting:

Proof.

(of
2.3.1
)
We may reduce to the case where
X
is affine.
Since all functors in discussion commute with sifted colimits, we may reduce to the case that
\mathcal{F}
is given by
L_{\mot}(Y)
for some
Y
that is affine and pro-étale over
\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X}
for some
n
by
2.1.7
and
[
BS15
, Theorem 2.3.4]
.
Furthermore all functors in discussion preserve motivic equivalences (the only non-trivial case is
i_{*}
but this is
2.2.6
).
This means that it suffices to see that the induced map

Y\amalg_{Y_{U}}U\rightarrow i_{*}Y_{Z}

is a motivic equivalence in
\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{X})
.
For this it suffices to see that for any affine
X
-scheme
f\colon T\to X
with a morphism
T\rightarrow i_{*}Y_{Z}
corresponding to a morphism
t_{0}\colon T_{Z}\to Y_{Z}
, the induced morphism

(Y\amalg_{Y_{U}}U\rightarrow i_{*}Y_{Z})\times_{i_{*}Y_{Z}}T

is a motivic equivalence.

But using the projection formula in
\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{T})
(see
[
Lur09
, Proposition 6.3.5.11]
) this map is given by applying the functor
f_{\sharp}\colon\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{T})\rightarrow\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{X})
to the morphism

\Phi_{T}(Y_{T},t)\rightarrow*=T.

with
t\colon T_{Z}\to Y_{T}\times_{T}T_{Z}=Y_{Z}\times_{X}T
induced by
t_{0}
on the first factor and the inclusion
T_{Z}\to T
on the second factor.
Hence, we have reduced to showing that the presheaf
\Phi_{T}(Y_{T},t)
is motivically contractible.
Recall that since
Y
was affine pro-étale over
\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X}
, it follows that the structure map
Y_{T}\rightarrow T
factors as

Y_{T}\xrightarrow{f_{T}}\mathbb{A}^{n}_{T}\rightarrow T

where all schemes are affine and
f_{T}
is pro-étale.
By Lemma
2.3.8
we may reduce to showing that
\Phi_{T}(\mathbb{A}^{n}_{T},(f_{T}\times_{T}T_{Z})\circ t)
is motivically contractible in
\operatorname{\mathrm{PSh}}(\mathrm{Sch}_{T})
, which is well-known as now
\mathbb{A}^{n}_{T}\rightarrow T
is smooth (see the proof of
[
Hoy17
, Theorem 4.18]
).
∎

This also implies the localization theorem for
\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
:

Corollary 2.3.9

(Pointed localization)
.

Let
i\colon Z\to X
be a closed immersion and let
j\colon U\to X
be the open complement. Assume that
j
is a qcqs immersion. For every
M\in\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(X)
The sequence

j_{\sharp}j^{*}M\to M\to i_{*}i^{*}M

is a cofiber sequence.
In particular
i^{*}
and
j^{*}
are jointly conservative and
i_{*}
is fully faithful.

Proof.

The first statement is a formal consequence of
2.3.1
, see
[
Hoy17
, Proposition 5.2]
.
That
i^{*}
and
j^{*}
are jointly conservative is an obvious consequence.
Plugging
i_{*}(-)
into the above cofiber sequence and using
j^{*}i_{*}\simeq\ast
we see that
i_{*}\to i_{*}i^{*}i_{*}
is an equivalence.
Thus the claim follows since
i_{*}
is conservative by
2.2.7
.
∎

Corollary 2.3.10

(Closed projection formula)
.

Let
i\colon Z\to X
be a closed immersion. Assume that its complement is a qcqs open immersion.
Let
M\in\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(X)
and let
N\in\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(Z)
. The canonical map

M\otimes i_{*}N\to i_{*}(i^{*}M\otimes N)

is an equivalence.

Proof.

This follows immediately from
2.3.9
using that
j^{*}i_{*}\simeq\ast
.
∎

2.3.11
.

Let
i\colon Z\to X
be a closed immersion.
Then canonical functor

\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(X)\otimes_{\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(Z)}\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(Z)\to\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)

is an equivalence by
[
AI22
, Lemma 1.3.11]
.

Corollary 2.3.12
.

Let
i\colon Z\to X
be a closed immersion. Assume that its complement is a qcqs open immersion.
Then the functor

i_{*}\colon\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(Z)\to\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)

commutes with colimits and the closed projection formula holds in
\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
.

Proof.

The closed projection formula for
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}
(
2.3.10
) together with
2.2.4
implies that the functor
i_{*}\colon\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(Z)\to\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(X)
is a map of
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(X)
-modules in
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}
(see
[
Lur22
, Remark 7.3.2.9]
).
Using
[
HSS17
, Section 4.4]
, as the adjunction
(i^{*},i_{*})
is internal to the
(\infty,2)
-category denoted by
\mathbf{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}(\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(X))
, whose objects are the presentable
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(X)
-modules, its image by the
(\infty,2)
-functor

-\otimes_{\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(Z)}\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(Z)\colon\mathbf{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}(\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t},\bullet}(X))\to\mathbf{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}(\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X))

is again an internal adjunction.
In particular, the right adjoint
i_{*}
of
i^{*}\colon\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)\to\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(Z)
preserves colimits and the projection formula holds.
∎

Corollary 2.3.13
.

Let
i\colon Z\to X
be a closed immersion and let
j\colon U\to X
be the open complement. Assume that
j
is a qcqs immersion.
For every
M\in\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
the sequence

j_{\sharp}j^{*}M\to M\to i_{*}i^{*}M

is a cofiber sequence. In particular the pair
(i^{*},j^{*})
is conservative.

Proof.

By
2.3.12
all functors in question commute with colimits and
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-stabilization.
Since
\Sigma^{\infty}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}
commutes with colimits and its image generates
\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
under colimits and
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-shifts, the claim follows from pointed localization (
2.3.9
).
∎

Corollary 2.3.14
.

Let
i\colon Z\to X
be a closed immersion.
Assume that its complement is a qcqs open immersion. Then the functor

i_{*}\colon\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(Z)\to\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)

is fully faithful.
The same holds for
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(Z)
and
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)
.

Proof.

The proof is the same as in
2.3.9
.
∎

2.4 Ambidexterity

In this section, we verify that no step of the proof of ambidexterity for smooth projective finitely presented morphism of qcqs schemes use a localization triangle with a non-qcqs open immersion. We follow
[
Hoy17
, Section 5]
.

Definition 2.4.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\colon\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\bullet})
be a functor with values in pointed symmetric monoidal presentable
\infty
-categories. For
f
a map of schemes, we denote by
f^{*}
the functor
\mathcal{C}(f)
and by
f_{*}
its right adjoint.
We say that
\mathcal{C}
is an
unstable qcqs motivic coefficient system
if the following conditions hold:

1.

For every smooth map
f
, the functor
f^{*}
has a left adjoint
f_{\sharp}
satisfying smooth base change and the smooth projection formula.

2.

\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariance
: the functor
\pi^{*}
is fully faithful if
\pi\colon\mathbb{A}^{1}_{X}\to X
is the canonical projection with
X
any qcqs scheme.

3.

Nisnevich descent
: the functor
\mathcal{C}
is a Nisnevich sheaf.

4.

Qcqs localization
: the analogue of
2.3.9
holds in
\mathcal{C}
.

Theorem 2.4.2

(Drew-Gallauer)
.

Let
\mathrm{H}_{\bullet}(-)
denote the unstable qcqs motivic coefficient system constructed by Morel and Voevodsky (it is the Nisnevich version, see
[
MV99
]
). There exists a natural transformation

\rho_{\mathcal{C}}\colon\mathrm{H}_{\bullet}(-)\to\mathcal{C}(-)

that also commutes with
f_{\sharp}
for a smooth morphism
f
.

Proof.

Indeed, this is a direct application of
[
DG22
, Proposition 6.8 and Theorem 7.3]
(see Remark 7.11 of
loc. cit.
).
∎

Lemma 2.4.3
.

Let
i\colon Z\to X
be a closed immersion with qcqs open complement and with
X
qcqs. Then the natural transformation

\rho_{\mathcal{C}}\circ i_{*}\to i_{*}\circ\rho_{\mathcal{C}}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

By localization, this follows directly from the commutation of
\rho_{\mathcal{C}}
with
j_{\sharp}
with
j\colon U\to X
the open complement.
∎

2.4.4
.

Let
S
be a qcqs scheme and let
\mathcal{M}
be a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-module. We denote by

p\colon\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{M})\to S
the associated vector bundle, with zero section
s\colon S\to\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{M})
.
Recall that one denotes by

\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}}\colon\mathcal{C}(S)\to\mathcal{C}(S)

the functor
p_{\sharp}s_{*}
. We also denote by
\mathrm{S}^{\mathcal{M}}\coloneq\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}}\mathbbm{1}_{S}\in\mathcal{C}(S)
. Note that the morphism
\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{M})\to S
is affine thus
\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{M})
is qcqs, and as the zero section is given by a finite type ideal (namely,
\mathcal{M}^{\vee}
), the open immersion
\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{M})\setminus S\to\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{M})
is a qcqs open immersion. By the closed projection formula (the analog of
2.3.10
which is follows formally from qcqs localization and the smooth projection formula), we then have that

\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}}\simeq\mathrm{S}^{\mathcal{M}}\otimes(-).

(3)

Moreover, by
2.4.3
we have
\rho_{\mathcal{C}}\circ\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}}\simeq\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}}\circ\rho_{\mathcal{C}}
.
If
f\colon S\to T
is smooth, then we denote by

\mathrm{Th}_{S}(\mathcal{M})\coloneqq f_{\sharp}(\mathrm{S}^{\mathcal{M}})\in\mathcal{C}(T)

the Thom space of
\mathcal{M}
. Again by
2.4.3
, we have that
\rho_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathrm{Th}_{S}(\mathcal{M}))\simeq\mathrm{Th}_{S}(\mathcal{M})
.

Theorem 2.4.5

(Purity)
.

Let

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

be a commutative diagram of qcqs schemes with
p
and
q
smooth and
s
a finitely presented closed immersion. Let
\mathcal{N}_{s}
be the normal bundle of
s
. Then there is a canonical isomorphism

p_{\sharp}s_{*}\simeq q_{\sharp}\Sigma^{\mathcal{N}_{s}}

of functors
\mathcal{C}(Z)\to\mathcal{C}(S)
.

Proof.

The morphism is obtained as a span given by deformation to the normal cone: there is a smooth morphism
\hat{p}\colon D_{Z}X\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{S}
with
D_{Z}X
the deformation space and a finitely presented closed immersion
\hat{s}\colon\mathbb{A}^{1}_{Z}\to D_{Z}X
obtained by pulling back the finitely presented closed immersion
Z\to X
along the structure map
D_{Z}X\to X
, such that the fiber at
1
is the triangle of the theorem and the fiber at
0
is the triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{N}_{s})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{0}}

with
s_{0}
the zero section of the normal bundle and
p_{0}
the canonical map. This induces a span

p_{\sharp}s_{*}\to r_{\sharp}\hat{p}_{\sharp}\hat{s}_{*}r^{*}\leftarrow q_{\sharp}\Sigma^{\mathcal{N}_{s}}

of functors
\mathcal{C}(Z)\to\mathcal{C}(S)
, with
r
the projection
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{(-)}\to(-)
(see the discussion above
[
Hoy17
, Proposition 5.7]
for more details). Using the closed projection formula (
2.3.10
which holds for
\mathcal{C}
as before), if
M\in\mathcal{C}(S)
, then the above span evaluated at
s^{*}M
can be written

p_{\sharp}s_{*}(\mathbbm{1}_{Z})\otimes M\to r_{\sharp}\hat{p}_{\sharp}\hat{s}_{*}r^{*}(\mathbbm{1}_{Z})\otimes M\leftarrow q_{\sharp}\Sigma^{\mathcal{N}_{s}}(\mathbbm{1}_{Z})\otimes M.

As
s_{*}
is fully faithful by
2.3.14
, in fact all objects of
\mathcal{C}(Z)
are of the form
s^{*}M
and it suffices to prove that the two morphism in the span are equivalences when evaluated at
\mathbbm{1}_{Z}
. Using
2.4.3
, it suffices to prove it in
\mathrm{H}_{\bullet}(S)
, where it is
[
Hoy17
, Proposition 5.7]
.
∎

Construction 2.4.6
.

In
[
Hoy17
, Section 5.3]
, Hoyois constructs the unit and counit of the ambidexterity theorem. We need the following. Let
f\colon X\to S
be a finitely presented projective smooth morphism of qcqs schemes. Then there exists (depending of some choice of an embedding of
X
in a projective space over
S
) a map (called the Pontryagin–Thom collapse)

\eta\colon\mathrm{S}^{\mathcal{M}}\to\mathrm{Th}_{X}(\mathcal{N})

in
\mathrm{H}_{\bullet}(S)
; here
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{N}
are vector bundles on
S
and
X
that satisfy
\mathrm{S}^{\Omega_{f}}\otimes\mathrm{S}^{\mathcal{N}}\simeq\mathrm{S}^{f^{*}(\mathcal{M})}
with
\Omega_{f}
the locally free sheaf of Kähler differentials. By
Equation

3
and the smooth projection formula, this defines a natural transformation

\eta\colon\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}}\simeq\mathrm{S}^{\mathcal{M}}\otimes(-)\xrightarrow{\rho_{\mathcal{C}}(\eta)\otimes(-)}\mathrm{Th}_{X}(\mathcal{N})\otimes(-)\simeq f_{\sharp}\Sigma^{\mathcal{N}}f^{*}

of functors
\mathcal{C}(S)\to\mathcal{C}(S)
.

He also defines a map (
[
Hoy17
, (5.20)]
)
\varepsilon\colon f^{*}f_{\sharp}\to\Sigma^{\Omega_{f}}
in
\mathrm{H}_{\bullet}(X)
and we can copy his definition to get a map in
\mathcal{C}(X)
as follows:

\varepsilon\colon f^{*}f_{\sharp}\simeq(p_{2})_{\sharp}p_{1}^{*}\to(p_{2})_{\sharp}\delta_{*}\delta^{*}p_{1}^{*}\simeq(p_{2})_{\sharp}\delta_{*}\overset{\text{\ref{purity}}}{\simeq}\Sigma^{\mathcal{N}_{\delta}}\overset{\nu}{\simeq}\Sigma^{\Omega_{f}},

with
p_{1},p_{2}\colon X\times_{S}X\to X
the projections,
\delta\colon X\to X\times_{S}X
the diagonal,
\nu\colon\mathcal{N}_{\delta}\to\Omega_{f}
the isomorphism sending
x\otimes 1-1\otimes x
to
dx
, and the one before last isomorphism is purity
2.4.5
, that we may apply because the morphism
X\to S
is of finite type so that the diagonal
\delta
is finitely presented by
[
Aut18
,
Tag 0818
]
.

Theorem 2.4.7

(Ambidexterity)
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a finitely presented projective smooth morphism of qcqs schemes. Then the compositions

f^{*}\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}}\xrightarrow{\eta}f^{*}f_{\sharp}\Sigma^{\mathcal{N}}f^{*}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}\Sigma^{\Omega_{f}}\Sigma^{\mathcal{N}}f^{*}\simeq\Sigma^{f^{*}(\mathcal{M})}f^{*}\simeq f^{*}\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}}

and

\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}}f_{\sharp}\xrightarrow{\eta}f_{\sharp}\Sigma^{\mathcal{N}}f^{*}f_{\sharp}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}f_{\sharp}\Sigma^{\mathcal{N}}\Sigma^{\Omega_{f}}\simeq f_{\sharp}\Sigma^{f^{*}(\mathcal{M})}\simeq\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}}f_{\sharp}

are the identity in
\mathcal{C}
.

In particular, if
\Sigma^{\mathcal{V}}
is an equivalence in
\mathcal{C}(S)
for any vector bundle
\mathcal{V}
, then the map
\varepsilon
induces a map
f^{*}f_{\sharp}\Sigma^{-\Omega_{f}}\to\mathrm{Id}
that exhibits
f^{*}
as the left adjoint of
f_{\sharp}\Sigma^{-\Omega_{f}}
and yields a natural equivalence
f_{\sharp}\Sigma^{-\Omega_{f}}\xrightarrow{\sim}f_{*}
.

Proof.

As in the proof of
[
Hoy17
, Theorem 5.20]
, if
\iota
is the first map evaluated at the unit, then the two maps are
\iota\otimes f^{*}(-)
and
f_{\sharp}(\iota\otimes(-))
thus it suffices to prove that
\iota
is the identity. By construction we have
\iota=\rho_{\mathcal{C}}(\iota)
and by
[
Hoy17
, Theorem 5.20]
,
\iota
is the identity in
\mathrm{H}_{\bullet}(S)
. Thus the proof is finished as the second point is a direct consequence (see
[
Hoy17
, Theorem 6.9]
).
∎

Definition 2.4.8
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an unstable qcqs motivic coefficient system
2.4.1
. We say that
\mathcal{C}
is a
qcqs motivic coefficient system
if

1.

It takes values in stable presentably symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories.

2.

It is
Tate-stable
meaning that the Tate motive
T_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})}\coloneqq\mathrm{S}^{\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})}}
is
\otimes
-invertible.

Remark 2.4.9
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a qcqs motivic coefficient system. Using Tate-stability, the proof of
[
DG22
, Theorem 7.14]
can easily be adapted to prove that there is a map

\SH\to\mathcal{C}

where
\SH
is the coefficient system of Nisnevich motivic spectra. Let now
\mathcal{M}
be a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module; since in
\SH
the sphere
\mathrm{S}^{\mathcal{M}}
is
\otimes
-invertible by
[
Ayo07a
, Theorème 1.5.7]
, so is its image in
\mathcal{C}
which is the object we also denoted by
\mathrm{S}^{\mathcal{M}}
. Hence, the map
\Sigma^{\mathcal{M}}
is an equivalence.

Corollary 2.4.10

(Ambidexterity)
.

Working over a qcqs motivic coefficient system, let
f\colon X\to S
be a finitely presented projective smooth morphism of qcqs schemes. There is a natural equivalence
f_{\sharp}\Sigma^{-\Omega_{f}}\xrightarrow{\sim}f_{*}
.

2.5 The six functors for pro-étale motives

Because the localization property is only true for closed immersions with qcqs open immersion, we do not get a 6 functor formalism as good as for
\mathrm{SH}
. Fix a qcqs motivic coefficient system
\mathcal{C}
.

Proposition 2.5.1
.

Let
p\colon Y\to X
be a finitely presented proper map.

1.

Let

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

be a cartesian square of qcqs schemes with
j
a smooth map. Then the exchange map

j_{\sharp}p^{\prime}_{*}\to p_{*}j^{\prime}_{\sharp}

is invertible.

2.

The functor
p_{*}
commutes with colimits.

3.

Let

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

be a cartesian square of qcqs schemes. Then the natural transformation

f^{*}p_{*}\to p^{\prime}_{*}(f^{\prime})^{*}

is an equivalence.

4.

For any
M
in
\mathcal{C}(X)
and any
N
in
\mathcal{C}(Y)
, the natural map

(p_{*}M)\otimes N\to p_{*}(M\otimes p^{*}N)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The proof follows the proofs of
[
AGV22
, Proposition 4.1.1, Proposition 4.1.2]
.
We first assume that
p
is projective. As all claims are local on
X
we may assume that
p=\pi\circ i
with
\pi\colon\mathbb{P}^{n}_{X}\to X
the projection and
i\colon Y\to X
a finitely presented closed immersion. We may treat
i
and
\pi
separately. The statements for
i_{*}
follow from localization (and
2.3.10
). Using ambidexterity we have
\pi_{*}\simeq\pi_{\sharp}\Sigma^{-\Omega}
, thus the four statements for
\pi_{*}
follow from their analogue for
\pi_{\sharp}
, which are clear.

We now reduce to the projective case.
We begin by noting that by Zariski descent, it suffices to prove that each qcqs open immersion
\nu\colon V\to Y
with base change
\nu^{\prime}
to
Y^{\prime}
, the map

j_{\sharp}p^{\prime}_{*}\circ\nu^{\prime}_{\sharp}\to p_{*}j^{\prime}_{\sharp}\circ\nu^{\prime}_{\sharp}
is an equivalence (1.), the functor
p_{*}\circ\nu_{\sharp}
preserves colimits (2.),
f^{*}p_{*}\circ\nu_{\sharp}\to p^{\prime}_{*}(f^{\prime})^{*}\circ\nu_{\sharp}
is an equivalence (3.) and
(p_{*}\circ\nu_{\sharp}M)\otimes N\to(p_{*}\circ\nu_{\sharp})(M\otimes\nu^{*}p^{*}N)
is an equivalence (4., here we now have
M\in\mathcal{C}(V)
). We fix such a
\nu\colon V\to Y
.
By the refined Chow’s lemma
[
Con07
, Corollary 2.6]
we may find a blow-up
e\colon Z\to Y
with center disjoint from
V
such that
q=p\circ e
is a projective morphism.
We let
w\colon V\to Z
be an open immersion such that
\nu=e\circ w
(this exists because
e
is an isomorphism above
V
). We define
Z^{\prime}=Z\times_{Y}Y^{\prime}
and
e^{\prime}\colon Z^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
,
q^{\prime}\colon Z^{\prime}\to X^{\prime}
and
w^{\prime}\colon V^{\prime}\to Z^{\prime}
to be the base changes of
e
,
q
and
w
along
f
.

We claim that
\nu_{\sharp}\simeq e_{*}\circ w_{\sharp}
and
\nu^{\prime}_{\sharp}\simeq e^{\prime}_{*}\circ w^{\prime}_{\sharp}
. The proof of the claim is the same in both cases thus we only deal with the first one.
Note first that there exists a comparison map
\alpha
obtained by adjunction from

\mathrm{Id}\to\nu^{*}e_{*}w_{\sharp}\simeq w^{*}w_{\sharp}
. Let
i\colon(Y\setminus V)_{\mathrm{red}}\to Y
be the reduced closed immersion. By localization, the pair
(i^{*},\nu^{*})
is conservative. We have
\nu^{*}(\alpha)=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathrm{\nu^{*}\nu_{\sharp}}}
by smooth base change. We have
i^{*}(\alpha)=\mathrm{Id}_{0}
by projective base change (that we proved in the first paragraph). This proves the claim.

Using the claim, we have:

1.

(j_{\sharp}p^{\prime}_{*}\circ\nu^{\prime}_{\sharp}\to p_{*}j^{\prime}_{\sharp}\circ\nu^{\prime}_{\sharp})=(j_{\sharp}(p^{\prime}\circ e^{\prime})_{*}\circ w^{\prime}_{\sharp}\to(p\circ e)\circ w_{\sharp}\circ j^{\prime\prime}_{\sharp})
with
j^{\prime\prime}
the base change of
j^{\prime}
to
U
.

2.

p_{*}\circ\nu_{\sharp}\simeq(p\circ e)_{*}\circ w_{\sharp}
.

3.

(f^{*}p_{*}\circ\nu_{\sharp}\to p^{\prime}_{*}(f^{\prime})^{*}\circ\nu_{\sharp})\simeq(f^{*}(p\circ e)_{*}\circ w_{\sharp}\to(p^{\prime}\circ e^{\prime})_{*}\circ w^{\prime}_{\sharp}\circ(f^{\prime\prime})^{*}
) with
f^{\prime\prime}
the base change of
f^{\prime}
to
V
.

4.

\displaystyle[(p_{*}\circ\nu_{\sharp}M)\otimes N\to(p_{*}\circ\nu_{\sharp})(M\otimes\nu^{*}p^{*}N)]

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle[((p\circ e)_{*}\circ w_{\sharp}M)\otimes N\to((p\circ e)_{*}\circ w_{\sharp})(M\otimes w^{*}(p\circ e)^{*}N)]

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle[(p\circ e)_{*}(w_{\sharp}M)\otimes N\to(p\circ e)_{*}((w_{\sharp}M)\otimes(p\circ e)^{*}N)]

using smooth projection formula for
w
.

In particular, we may replace
p
and
p^{\prime}
by
q
and
q^{\prime}
thus reducing to the case where
p
is a finitely presented projective map.

∎

2.5.2
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a separated finitely presented morphism of qcqs schemes. Then, there is a finitely presented compactification of
f

i.e.
a finitely presented proper morphism
\overline{f}\colon\overline{X}\to S
and a qcqs open immersion
j\colon X\to\overline{X}
such that
f=\overline{f}\circ j
.
To see that, we may write
S=\lim S_{i}
as a cofiltered limit of a system of Noetherian schemes with affine transition maps by
[
Aut18
,
Tag 01ZJ
]
. We can then choose an index
i
and a morphism
X_{i}\to S_{i}
of finite presentation whose base change to
S
is
X\to S
, by
[
Aut18
,
Tag 01ZM
]
. After increasing
i
we may assume
X_{i}\to S_{i}
is separated, by
[
Aut18
,
Tag 01ZQ
]
.
But we can find a compactification of
X_{i}
over
S_{i}
by
[
Aut18
,
Tag 0F41
]
; it is then finitely presented and the base change of this to
S
is a compactification of
X
.

In the above setting, we can set
f_{!}\coloneqq\overline{f}_{*}j_{\sharp}
. Note that this functor preserves colimits: it is clear for
j_{\sharp}
and follows from
2.4.10
for
\overline{f}_{*}
.
We can then follow
[
AGV22
, § 4.3]
to upgrade
f\mapsto f_{!}
into a functor
\mathcal{C}_{!}\colon\mathrm{Sch}_{\mathrm{qcqs}}^{\mathrm{fp}}\to\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}
from the category of qcqs schemes with finitely presented (non-necessarily separated) morphisms. In fact we can do better (see
2.5.4
).

The following extension of
2.4.5
and
2.4.7
is classical:

Corollary 2.5.3
.

1.

Let
f\colon Y\to X
be a smooth map of qcqs schemes. Then there is a canonical isomorphism
f^{!}\simeq f^{*}\Sigma^{\Omega_{f}}
with
\Omega_{f}
the sheaf of differentials of
f
.

2.

Let
f\colon Y\to X
be a finitely presented smooth and proper map. Then there is a natural equivalence
f_{\sharp}\Sigma^{-\Omega_{f}}\xrightarrow{\sim}f_{*}
.

Proof.

The second point readily follows from the first, using that
f_{!}\simeq f_{*}
for a proper map. The proof of the first is classical, see for example
[
Kha21
, Theorem 2.44]
(one checks similarly as before that the proof does not use non qcqs open immersions).
∎

Theorem 2.5.4
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\colon\mathrm{Sch}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}})
be a qcqs motivic coefficient system. Then it is endowed with the six functors. More precisely, this means:

1.

There exists a lax-symmetric monoidal functor

\mathcal{C}_{!}^{*}\colon\mathrm{Corr}(\mathrm{Sch},\mathrm{fp})^{\otimes}\to\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\otimes}

extending
\mathcal{C}
, where
\mathrm{Corr}(\mathrm{Sch},\mathrm{fp})
is the category of correspondences defined in
[
HM24
, Definition 2.2.10]
(see
2.5.5
), with backward maps any map, and onward maps finitely presented morphisms of qcqs schemes. Roughly, this means that we have all the higher coherence for the following:

•

For each finitely presented map of qcqs schemes
f
, we have a functor
f_{!}
, which is compatible with the formation of pullbacks.

•

We have a natural isomorphism

(f_{!}M)\otimes N\to f_{!}(M\otimes f^{*}N).

•

If
f
is finitely presented proper, then
f_{!}
is the right adjoint of
f^{*}
, and if
f
is a qcqs open immersion, then
f_{!}
is the left adjoint of
f^{*}
.

2.

For any
f\colon X\to S
finitely presented smooth morphism of qcqs schemes, there is a natural equivalence
f^{!}\simeq\Sigma^{\Omega_{f}}\circ f^{*}
.

3.

For any closed immersion
i\colon Z\to X
with qcqs open complement
j\colon X\setminus Z\to X
, we have exact triangles of natural transformations

j_{!}j^{!}\to\mathrm{Id}\to i_{*}i^{*}

i_{!}i^{!}\to\mathrm{Id}\to j_{*}j^{*}

given by the unit and counit of the appropriate adjunctions.

4.

There is a natural transformation
f_{!}\to f_{*}
which is an equivalence if
f
is proper of finite presentation.

Proof.

Let
\mathrm{Corr}(\mathrm{Sch},\mathrm{fp}^{\mathrm{sep}})
be the category of correspondences whose objects are qcqs schemes, backward morphisms are any morphisms, and onward morphisms are finitely presented separated maps (see
[
HM24
, Definition 2.2.10]
). The monoidal structure is induced by the cartesian structure on schemes.
We first apply
[
CLL25
, Theorem 4.35]
, using
2.5.1
,
2.5.1
and
2.5.2
. This provides a lax symmetric monoidal
2
-functor

\mathbf{\mathcal{C}}^{*}_{!}\colon\mathrm{SPAN}_{2}(\mathrm{Sch},\mathrm{fp}^{\mathrm{sep}})^{\otimes}_{\mathrm{prop,open}}\to\mathbf{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\otimes},

where
\mathrm{SPAN}_{2}(\mathrm{Sch},\mathrm{fp}^{\mathrm{sep}})^{\otimes}_{\mathrm{prop,open}}
is a
(2,2)
-category of correspondences defined in
[
CLL25
, Construction 4.1]
(in the notations of
loc. cit.
we take
C
the category of qcqs schemes,
E
the class of finitely presented separated maps,
I
the class of qcqs open immersions and
P
the class of finitely presented proper maps) and
\mathbf{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}
is the
(\infty,2)
-category of presentable
\infty
-categories, endowed with the Lurie tensor product. The underlying
(2,1)
-category of this
(2,2)
-category is the category
\mathrm{Corr}(\mathrm{Sch},\mathrm{fp}^{\mathrm{sep}})
by
[
CLL25
, Lemma 4.13]
. In particular, by composing with the canonical lax monoidal inclusion

\mathrm{Corr}(\mathrm{Sch},\mathrm{fp}^{\mathrm{sep}})^{\otimes}\to\mathrm{SPAN}_{2}(\mathrm{Sch},\mathrm{fp}^{\mathrm{sep}})^{\otimes}_{\mathrm{prop,open}}

and forgetting the
2
-morphisms, we obtain a lax symmetric monoidal
\infty
-functor

\mathcal{C}^{*}_{!}\colon\mathrm{Corr}(\mathrm{Sch},\mathrm{fp}^{\mathrm{sep}})^{\otimes}\to\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\otimes}

which is almost all we wanted. The claim was about having for
!
-able maps all finitely presented and not only separated thus we have to do some Zariski descent. Note that for any finitely presented map
f\colon Y\to X
, there exists an hypercovering
Y_{\bullet}\to Y
for the Zariski topology such that each composite
Y_{n}\to Y\to X
is separated (for example, we may take the
Y_{n}
to be affine). We now use the terminology introduced in
[
HM24
, Definition 4.5.1]
. By definition, for any Zariski covering
j\colon U\to S
, we have
j^{*}\simeq j^{!}
, where
j^{!}
is the right adjoint of
j_{!}
. Thus by
[
HM24
, Lemma 4.5.4]
, the map
f
is
\mathcal{C}
-suave. Moreover, by Zariski descent, the functor
j^{*}
is conservative, thus by
[
HM24
, Lemma 4.7.1]
, the map
j
is a universal
\mathcal{C}^{!}
-cover. All of this put together allows us to apply
[
HM24
, Proposition 3.4.8 (ii)]
to conclude that the functor
\mathcal{C}^{*}_{!}
extends uniquely to a 6-functor formalism on
\mathrm{Corr}(\mathrm{Sch},\mathrm{fp})
the category of correspondences where now all finitely presented maps are onward maps: this provides a lax symmetric monoidal functor

\mathcal{C}^{*}_{!}\colon\mathrm{Corr}(\mathrm{Sch},\mathrm{fp})^{\otimes}\to\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\otimes}

as promised.

The second point is
2.4.10
, the third point is an assumption on
\mathcal{C}
and the fourth point follows from the third: given a factorization
f=p\circ j
, applying the localization triangle to
j_{*}
and further applying
p_{*}
, we obtain the natural transformation.
∎

Remark 2.5.5
.

The category
\mathrm{Corr}(\mathrm{Sch},\mathrm{fp})
has all qcqs schemes for objects. If
X
and
Y
are qcqs, the morphisms
X\to Y
in the category of or correspondences are

\alpha\colon X\xleftarrow{f}Z\xrightarrow{g}Y

where
f
is any morphism and
g
is a finitely presented morphism. Composition is given by the obvious pullback diagram. The functor
\mathcal{C}^{*}_{!}
we obtain has the additional property that
\mathcal{C}^{*}_{!}(\alpha)\simeq g_{!}f^{*}
. If
g
is furthermore separated, then for any compactification
g=p\circ j
with
p
finitely presented proper and
j
a qcqs open immersion, we have
g_{!}\simeq p_{*}j_{!}
. If
g
is not separated anymore, one can choose a Zariski hypercovering
Z_{\bullet}\to Z
by affines, and by definition, if one denote by
g^{n}\colon Z_{n}\to Y
the composition
Z_{n}\to Z\to Y
of
g
with the morphism
j_{n}\colon Z_{n}\to Z
, we have:

g_{!}\simeq\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{n\in\Delta}g^{n}_{!}j_{n}^{*}.

Corollary 2.5.6
.

We have the six functors on
\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
in the sense of
2.5.4
.

2.6 The embedding theorem from étale motives

If
\Lambda
is a condensed ring spectrum, it can be seen as an object of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Sp)
by using the canonical maps of sites

*_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\to X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}\to(\mathrm{WSm}_{X})_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}.

We let

\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,\Lambda)\coloneqq\mathrm{Mod}_{\Lambda}(\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X)).

If the condensed ring spectrum
\Lambda
is
H\mathbb{Z}
-linear, we denote by
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,\Lambda)
the
\infty
-category
\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,\Lambda)
and call it the category of
pro-étale motives with
\Lambda
-coefficients
; in that case, we also denote by
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)
the category
\mathrm{Mod}_{\Lambda}(\mathrm{SH}^{S^{1}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X))
.

Definition 2.6.1
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme.

1.

We say that
X
is étale bounded if it has finite Krull dimension and if

\sup_{x\in X,p\text{ prime}}\mathrm{cd}_{p}(k(x))<+\infty

where
\mathrm{cd}_{p}(k)
is the mod
p
Galois cohomological dimension of a field
k
.

2.

We say that
X
is locally étale bounded if it is étale-locally étale bounded.

Theorem 2.6.2
.

Let
X
be a locally étale bounded qcqs scheme.
Then for any ring spectrum
\Lambda
(endowed with the discrete topology), the canonical functor

\SH_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(S,\Lambda)\to\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(S,\Lambda)

is fully faithful.

Proof.

As the
\Lambda
-linear version is obtained by taking
\Lambda
-modules on both sides, we only deal with spectral coefficients. We can reduce to étale bounded schemes because both
\SH_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
and
\SH_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
have étale descent.

We begin by showing that the functor

\nu^{*}\colon\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Sm}_{X})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X})

is fully faithful, that is we want to see that the canonical map

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Sm}_{X})}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\to\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X})}(\nu^{*}\mathcal{F},\nu^{*}\mathcal{G})

is an equivalence.
We may assume that
\mathcal{F}=T
for a smooth
X
-scheme
T
.
Then the map may be rewritten as

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Sm}_{T})}(\mathbbm{1},f^{*}\mathcal{G})\to\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{T})}(\mathbbm{1},f^{*}\nu^{*}\mathcal{G})

Since the unit is in the essential image of the inclusion functors from the small étale sites we may again identify this as

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sh}(T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})}(\mathbbm{1},\theta f^{*}\mathcal{G})\to\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sh}({T}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})}(\mathbbm{1},\theta^{\prime}\nu^{*}f^{*}\mathcal{G})

where the functors
\theta
and
\theta^{\prime}
are the right adjoints to the inclusion from the small étale site.
Furthermore we have a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}(T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}(T_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Sm}_{T})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{T})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta^{\prime}}

(all functors preserve colimits because of
[
CM21
, Proposition 7.1]
and on representables this is easy).
So we may reduce to
\nu^{*}
being fully faithful on the small site, which follows from Postnikov-completeness of both sides (which is a consequence of
[
BS15
, Proposition 3.2.3]
on the pro-étale site and of our assumption on finite cohomological dimension for the étale site, see
[
Mat25
, Lemma 2.23]
) and the truncated case which is
[
HHL+25
, Proposition 2.38]
.

The stable analogue of
\nu^{*}
is thus also fully faithful.
We now claim that it restricts to a fully faithful functor

\mathrm{SH}^{S^{1}}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X)\to\mathrm{SH}^{S^{1}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X).

In other words we have to show that if
\mathcal{F}
is an
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant sheaf of spectra on
\mathrm{Sm}_{X}
, then
\nu^{*}\mathcal{F}
is also
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant.
We first claim that
\nu^{*}
is t-exact for the standard t-structure: as restricting to small sites is t-exact and commutes with
\nu^{*}
, we are reduced to showing that
\nu^{*}\colon\mathrm{Sh}(T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathrm{Sp})\to\mathrm{Sh}(T_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathrm{Sp})
is t-exact for any smooth
X
-scheme
T
. The result then follows from
[
Lura
, Remark 1.3.2.8]
.

So let
U=\lim_{i}U_{i}
be affine and pro-étale over some smooth
X
-scheme
T
.
We want to show that

(\nu^{*}\mathcal{F})(U)\to(\nu^{*}\mathcal{F})(U\times\mathbb{A}^{1})

is an equivalence.
Since
\nu^{*}\mathcal{F}\simeq\lim_{n}\tau_{\leq n}\nu^{*}\mathcal{F}
by Postnikov-completeness of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X})
(which is again a consequence of
[
BS15
, Proposition 3.2.3]
) and because
\nu^{*}
commutes with truncations, we may assume that
\mathcal{F}=\tau_{\leq n}\mathcal{F}
.
But then the above map rewrites as

\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}(\nu^{*}\mathcal{F})(U_{i})\to\operatorname{\mathrm{colim}}_{i}(\nu^{*}\mathcal{F})(U_{i}\times_{X}\mathbb{A}^{1}_{X});

indeed, the formula for the left-hand side can be checked by restricting to
\mathrm{Sh}(T_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})
and the formula for the right-hand side can be checked by restricting to
\mathrm{Sh}((\mathbb{A}^{1}_{T})_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})
and in both cases, the argument is then the same as in
[
BS15
, Corollary 5.1.6]
which is the case of the derived category of sheaves of abelian groups. Thus our map is an equivalence.

Now the claim of the theorem follows formally, if we can show that the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{SH}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{S^{1}}(X)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{SH}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}^{S^{1}}(X)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{SH}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{S^{1}}(X)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{SH}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}^{S^{1}}(X)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathbb{S}[\mathbb{G}_{m}],-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathbb{S}[\mathbb{G}_{m}],-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu^{*}}

commutes in the obvious way.
That is we have to show that the canonical map

\nu^{*}\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathbb{S}[\mathbb{G}_{m}],\mathcal{F})\to\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathbb{S}[\mathbb{G}_{m}],\nu^{*}\mathcal{F})

is an equivalence for any
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{SH}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{S^{1}}(X)
.
Note that here the internal Homs a priori are taken in
\mathrm{SH}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{S^{1}}(X)
and
\mathrm{SH}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}^{S^{1}}(X)
, respectively, but the latter in fact agree with the internal Homs in
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Sm}_{X},\Sp)
and
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Sp)
, respectively.
Thus we may argue in the categories of sheaves, instead of effective motives.
Then the claim follows from
2.6.3
and
2.6.4
below (indeed note that
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathbb{S}[Y],-)
, for
f\colon Y\to X
a smooth map, is isomorphic to
f_{*}f^{*}
by adjunction and smooth projection formula).
∎

Proposition 2.6.3
.

Let
S
be an étale bounded qcqs scheme.
Let
f\colon T\to S
be a map of schemes such that for any base change
f^{\prime}\colon X\times_{S}T\to X
to a smooth
S
-scheme
X
, the functor
f_{*}\colon\mathrm{Sh}((X\times_{S}T)_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z})
has finite cohomological dimension.
Then the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Sm}_{T},\Sp)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{T},\Sp)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Sm}_{S},\Sp)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{S},\Sp)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu^{*}}

commutes.

Proof.

For any smooth map
g\colon X\to S
, we consider the cube

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}((X\times_{S}T)_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\Sp)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}((X\times_{S}T)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Sp)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\Sp)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Sp)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Sm}_{T},\Sp)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{T},\Sp)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Sm}_{S},\Sp)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{S},\Sp)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu^{*}}

where the diagonal functors are given by base changing and then restricting.
Note that the left-hand square and the right-hand square of the cube clearly commute.
The top and bottom already commute at the level of sheaves of spaces as we have seen in the proof of the theorem.
Since the collection of all functors of the form
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{S},\mathrm{Sp})\to\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\mathrm{Sp})
for all smooth
g\colon X\to S
is conservative, we may reduce to showing that the back square commutes.
We observe that for bounded above sheaves of spectra, this follows easily by adapting the proof of
[
BS15
, Lemma 5.4.3]
.

To extend to all sheaves of spectra, note that the finiteness assumptions imply that the horizontal functors are fully faithful.
It therefore suffices to see that
f^{\prime}_{*}(\nu^{*}\mathcal{F})
is in the image of
\nu^{*}
: indeed then,
f^{\prime}_{*}(\nu^{*}\mathcal{F})=\nu^{*}\nu_{*}(f^{\prime}_{*}(\nu^{*}\mathcal{F}))
,

\nu_{*}(f^{\prime}_{*}(\nu^{*}\mathcal{F}))(U)=f^{\prime}_{*}(\nu^{*}\mathcal{F})(U)=\nu^{*}\mathcal{F}(f^{\prime-1}(U))=\mathcal{F}(f^{\prime-1}(U))=f^{\prime}_{*}(\mathcal{F})(U)

for any
U
étale over
X
.
Now, observe that we may identify the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,\Sp)
spanned by the image of
\nu^{*}
with the full subcategory spanned by those proétale sheaves whose homotopy sheaves are classical by
[
BH21
, Lemma 3.5]
.
It therefore remains to see that
\pi_{k}(f^{\prime}_{*}(\nu^{*}\mathcal{F}))\in\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},\Sp)^{\heartsuit}
is classical.
By Postnikov-completeness, we note that

f^{\prime}_{*}(\nu^{*}\mathcal{F})=\lim_{n}f^{\prime}_{*}(\nu^{*}\tau_{\leq n}\mathcal{F}).

It follows that the inverse system
\left(\pi_{k}f^{\prime}_{*}(\nu^{*}\tau_{\leq n}\mathcal{F})\right)_{k}
is eventually constant since the connectivity of the fiber of

\nu^{*}f^{\prime}_{*}(\pi_{n}\mathcal{F})=f^{\prime}_{*}(\nu^{*}\pi_{n}\mathcal{F})=\mathrm{fib}(f^{\prime}_{*}(\nu^{*}\tau_{\leq n}\mathcal{F})\to f^{\prime}_{*}(\nu^{*}\tau_{\leq n-1}\mathcal{F}))

goes to infinity as
n
grows, by our cohomological finiteness assumption.
Thus for
n>\!\!>0
we have
\pi_{k}f^{\prime}_{*}(\nu^{*}\mathcal{F})=\pi_{k}f^{\prime}_{*}(\nu^{*}\tau_{\leq n}\mathcal{F})
and the latter is classical since
\tau_{\leq n}\mathcal{F}
is bounded above.
∎

Lemma 2.6.4
.

Let
S
be an étale bounded qcqs scheme.
Then any finite presentation morphism
f\colon X\to S
satisfies the assumptions of
2.6.3
.

Proof.

First note that if
T\to S
is any finitely presented map,
then
T
is also étale bounded by
[
Mat25
, Lemma 2.24]

(the statement is for smooth morphisms but the proof works for any finite presentation morphism). In particular it suffices to prove that the functor

Rf_{*}
has finite cohomological dimension.
Also, any étale bounded qcqs scheme of finite dimension has finite étale cohomological
dimension by
[
CM21
, Corollary 3.29]
, even better, for any
U\to X
étale, we have
\mathrm{DimCoh}(U)\leqslant\sup_{x\in X}\sup_{p\in\mathbb{P}}\mathrm{cd}_{p}(\kappa(x))+\mathrm{dim}(X)=:N
.
Finally, by
[
Aut18
,
Lemma 072W
]
, if
A\in\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z})
, the sheaf

R^{i}f_{*}A
is the sheafification of the presheaf that sends an étale scheme
V\to S
to
\mathrm{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(f^{-1}(V),A)
. In particular, for
i>N
, we have
R^{i}f_{*}A=0
, finishing the proof.
∎

Remark 2.6.5
.

2.6.2
also works if
X
is finite dimensional, and étale locally,

\sup_{x\in X,p\in\mathcal{P}}\mathrm{cd}_{p}(k(x))<+\infty

with
\mathcal{P}
the set of primes that are not invertible in
\Lambda
.
The proof works the same, replacing sheaves of spectra with sheaves of
\mathcal{P}
-local spectra.
Indeed, the analogue of
2.6.4
is still a consequence of
[
CM21
, Corollary 3.29]
and the Postnikov-completeness of objects in
\mathrm{Sh}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\Sp_{\mathcal{(P)}})
used in the proofs of
2.6.2
and
2.6.3
follows from
[
CM21
, Theorem 4.28]
.

Corollary 2.6.6
.

Over locally étale bounded qcqs schemes, étale motives have non-effective pro-étale hyperdescent. That is, if
U_{\bullet}\to X
is a pro-étale hypercovering with
X
and each
U_{n}
locally étale bounded, and if
M,N\in\mathrm{SH}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X)
, the map

\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{SH}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X)}(M,N)\to\lim_{[n]\in\Delta}\mathrm{map}_{\mathrm{SH}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(U_{n})}(M_{\mid U_{n}},N_{\mid U_{n}})

is an equivalence of spectra. For
M=\mathbb{S}
the unit and
N=H\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(i)
the twisted étale-local motivic Eilenberg-Mac Lane spectrum with
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, this means that étale motivic cohomology has pro-étale descent.

Proof.

This is true in
\mathrm{SH}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
by pro-étale descent, thus
2.6.2
implies the result.
∎

3 The rigidity theorem.

The main goal of this section is to prove a version of the
rigidity theorem
in our context, see
3.3.7
.
In
Section

3.1
, we define categories of
solid motives
by applying the abstract solidification procedure of
Section

1.4
to categories of pro-étale motives.
We prove that solid pro-étale sheaves embed fully faithfully into these categories in
Section

3.2
and prove a rigidity theorem with torsion coefficients
3.2.7
.
Finally in
Section

3.3
, we prove a version of the rigidity theorem with
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-coefficients.
In order to do so, we need to pass to a further localization of the categories introduced in
Section

3.1
, see
3.3.3
.
As a consequence of the rigidity theorem
3.3.7
, it follows that the categories solid pro-étale sheaves introduced in
Section

1
have the six operations.

In this section, we fix a solid
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-algebra
\Lambda
, where
\mathcal{P}
is a set of prime numbers, and a qcqs scheme
X
.

3.1 Solid motives.

Definition 3.1.1
.

We define condensed presentably monoidal
\infty
-categories by

\underline{\mathrm{D}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)\colon S\mapsto\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X\times S},\Lambda)

\underline{\mathrm{D}}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)\colon S\mapsto\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X\times S},\Lambda)

\underline{\mathrm{DM}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,\Lambda)\colon S\mapsto\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X\times S,\Lambda)

The
\infty
-category of
solid
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariant sheaves
over
X
is then defined as

\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\coloneqq\underline{\mathrm{D}}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}(*).

We also define a Tate-stable version, as

\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1},{\mathbb{G}_{m}}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\coloneqq\underline{\mathrm{DM}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}(*).

Finally, we set

\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\coloneqq\underline{\mathrm{D}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}(*).

Remark 3.1.2
.

All of the above categories coincide with
\Lambda
-modules in the analogous categories with
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
-coefficients.

Theorem 3.1.3
.

The functor
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{(-)},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\colon\mathrm{Sch}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}})
is an unstable qcqs motivic coefficient system.
More explicitly we have the following properties

1.

For every weakly smooth map
f\colon X\to Y
the functor
f^{*}
has a
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{Y},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
-linear left adjoint that is compatible with base change.

2.

For any scheme
X
the functor
\pr_{1}^{*}\colon\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
is fully faithful.

3.

For any closed immersion
i\colon Z\to X
with qcqs open complement
j\colon U\to X
, the canonical square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{U},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ast}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{Z},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{*}}

in
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}
is cocartesian.

The same is true for
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1},{\mathbb{G}_{m}}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{(-)},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
.

Proof.

Let us start by proving 1.
Extending the notations of
3.1.1
to arbitrary condensed
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra, we see that for a weakly smooth map
f\colon X\to S
we have a condensed adjunction

{f_{\sharp}}\colon{\underline{\mathrm{D}}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Sp)}\leftrightarrows{\underline{\mathrm{D}}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{S},\Sp)}\colon{f^{*}}

in
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\cond}
and the left adjoint
f_{\sharp}
is compatible with base change.
Using
1.4.12
, it follows that the functor
f^{*}\colon\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{S},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
has a left adjoint given by
f_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}=(f_{\sharp}\otimes^{\cond}\underline{\mathrm{Solid}}_{\Lambda})(*)
.
Thus, compatibility with base change follows immediately from the bifunctoriality of
-\otimes-
.
To see that
f_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}
satisfies the projection formula, we note that by
[
HHLN23
, Proposition A]
there is a canonical comparison map

f_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}(F\otimes f^{*}G)\to f_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}(F)\otimes G

and it just remains to show that it is an equivalence.
For this, we note that all the functors in question are given by applying their non-solid versions and then solidifying and thus the claim follows from the projection formula for
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Sp)
.

We now prove 2.
Similarly as before the adjunction
p_{\sharp}\dashv p^{*}
lifts to an adjunction in
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\cond}
where the right adjoint is fully faithful.
Thus the same is still true after applying
-\otimes^{\cond}\underline{\mathrm{Solid}}_{\Lambda}
using
1.4.11
.

Finally for 3., we note that we have an analogous cocartesian square in
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\cond}
before applying
-\otimes^{\cond}\mathrm{Solid}_{\Lambda}
by
2.3.1
.
Since the latter functor is a left adjoint it preserves cocartesian squares and the claim follows using
1.4.7
.
∎

3.2 Embedding solid sheaves into solid motives

We now prove that solid sheaves embed fully faithfully into the categories of solid motives that we consider.

Lemma 3.2.1
.

Consider the fully faithful functor of condensed categories

\rho_{\sharp}\colon\underline{\mathrm{Sh}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})\to\underline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X}).

Then this functor is a condensed right adjoint.

Proof.

By
1.4.5
, we have to show that for any profinite set
K
, the functor

\rho_{\sharp}\colon\mathrm{Sh}((X\times K)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X\times K})

preserves all limits and that for any map
\alpha\colon K\to K^{\prime}
of profinite sets, the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{Sh}}((X\times K^{\prime})_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X\times K^{\prime}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{Sh}}((X\times K)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X\times K})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{*}}

is horizontally left adjointable, or equivalently vertically right adjointable.

For this we consider the restriction functors to any small proétale site
\mathrm{Sh}(Y_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})
for
f\colon Y\to X\times K
weakly smooth and consider the composite squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{Sh}}((X\times K^{\prime})_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X\times K^{\prime}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{Sh}}((Y^{\prime})_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{Sh}}((X\times K)_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X\times K})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{Sh}}(Y_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{res}_{Y^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{res}_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{Y}^{*}}

where we write
Y^{\prime}=Y\times_{(X\times K^{\prime})}(X\times K)
.
Observe that the collection of functors
\mathrm{res}_{Y}
for all
Y
is jointly conservative.
Since
\mathrm{res}_{Y}
commutes with all limits and we have
\mathrm{res}_{Y}\circ\rho_{\sharp}\simeq f^{*}
, it follows that
\rho_{\sharp}
commutes with limits pointwise by
1.1.7
.
Similarly, it follows that we may check vertical right adjointability of left square above after applying
\mathrm{res}_{Y}
for all
Y
.
Now note that the right square is vertically right adjointable as it is clearly horizontally left adjointable.
Thus it suffices to show vertical right adjointability of the composite square.
Via the identification
\mathrm{res}_{Y}\circ\rho_{\sharp}\simeq f^{*}
, this follows from integral base change
[
HHL+25
, Proposition 6.18]
, as in the proof of
1.4.15
.
∎

Corollary 3.2.2
.

For any
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\cond}
, the functor

\underline{\mathrm{Sh}}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}})\otimes^{\cond}\mathcal{C}\to\underline{\mathrm{Sh}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X})\otimes^{\cond}\mathcal{C}

is a fully faithful condensed right adjoint.

Proof.

By
3.2.1
, the functor
\rho_{\sharp}
has a condensed left adjoint. The lemma then follows from
1.4.14
.∎

3.2.3
.

As a particular case of the above corollary we obtain a fully faithful and limit-preserving functor

\rho_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\colon\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}

by letting
\mathcal{C}=\underline{\mathrm{Solid}}_{\Lambda}
and evaluating at
*
.

Remark 3.2.4
.

Note that the canonical square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}},\Lambda)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{1}^{*}}

where the horizontal maps are the inclusions given by
1.4.14
commutes.
Indeed, the square obtained by taking left adjoints commutes, since
(\pr_{1})_{\sharp}
commutes with solidification.
It follows that
F\in\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
is in
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
if and only if the canonical map
F(Y)\to F(Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1})
is an equivalence for any weakly smooth
Y
over
X
.

Corollary 3.2.5
.

Assume that all primes in
\mathcal{P}
are invertible on
X
. Then, the fully faithful functor
\rho_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\colon\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
factors through the full subcategory
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
.

Proof.

We may assume that
\Lambda=\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
. Note that the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(X,{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}})^{\blacksquare}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}})^{\blacksquare}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}},{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\sharp}}

commutes, because it is obtained by tensoring an adjunction in
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\cond}
with
\underline{\mathrm{Solid}}_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}}
(see the proof of
1.4.17
).
Thus we have to check that for
F\in\mathrm{D}(X,{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}})^{\blacksquare}
the canonical map

\rho_{\sharp}(F)(Y)\to\rho_{\sharp}(F)(Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1})

is an equivalence for any
f\colon Y\to X\in\mathrm{WSm}_{X}
.
This map identifies with the canonical map

f^{*}F(Y)\to\pr_{1}^{*}f^{*}F(Y\times\mathbb{A}^{1})
, as
f^{*}F
is solid by
1.2.11
, we may assume that
Y=X
. As
\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{*}
commutes with limits by
1.2.11
, we can use Postnikov-completeness to assume that
F
is bounded below, and we can then further reduce to
F
being in the heart by a spectral sequence argument. Then, we can reduce to
F
being in
\mathrm{Pro}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}))
by
1.2.8
and then in
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathrm{cons}}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})
in which case it follows from
[
sga72
, Exposé XV, Corollaire 2.2]
.
∎

Theorem 3.2.6
.

Assume that all primes in
\mathcal{P}
are invertible on
X
. Then, the functor

\rho_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\colon\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}

of
3.2.5
is fully faithful, commutes with all small colimits and limits, all pullbacks and with
f_{\sharp}
for
f
weakly étale.

Proof.

The commutation with colimits is formal (we are taking the global sections of a map in
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathrm{cond}}
), the commutation with limits is
3.2.3
. The commutation with pullbacks and with
f_{\sharp}
for
f
weakly étale is true at the condensed level before solidification and taking global sections so that it remains true.
∎

From this, we can deduce the solid rigidity theorem with torsion coefficients.

Proposition 3.2.7
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
be invertible on
X
. Assume that
\Lambda
is a
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
-algebra.Then, the functors

\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1},{\mathbb{G}_{m}}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}

are all equivalences.

Proof.

It suffices to deal with the case
\Lambda=\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
.
Using the functor frome effective étale motives we see that in
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\blacksquare}
the Tate twist is already invertible, thus the functor

\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1},{\mathbb{G}_{m}}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\blacksquare}

is an equivalence. Hence, using
3.2.6
, the only thing left to prove is that the functor

\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}

is essentially surjective knowing that it is fully faithful. Hence, we only have to prove that collection of objects that generate the target category under colimits are in the image.
Now, the
f_{\sharp}(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})
for
f
weakly smooth generate
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\blacksquare}
. Zariski-locally on the source such an
f
can be written as a composition

U\xrightarrow{g}\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X}\xrightarrow{j}\mathbb{P}^{n}_{X}\xrightarrow{p}X

with
g
weakly étale,
j
the canonical open immersion and
p
the canonical projection (here we just use
2.1.7
and decompose the canonical projection
\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X}\to X
); as
j\circ g
is still weakly étale, we see that
f
is Zariski-locally on the source of the form

U\xrightarrow{h}\mathbb{P}^{n}_{X}\xrightarrow{p}X

with
h
weakly étale. Hence, the
p_{\sharp}h_{\sharp}(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})
for
p
and
h
as above generate
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\blacksquare}
.
But then, ambidexterity (
2.4.10
) ensures that
p_{\sharp}\simeq p_{*}(n)[2n]
and thus the
p_{*}h_{\sharp}(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})
generate
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\blacksquare}
.

Note now that on both sides we have proper base change for finitely presented morphism by
1.3.6
and
2.5.1
. This formally implies that
p_{*}
commutes with
\rho_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}
: indeed, we have an exchange morphism
\rho_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}p_{*}\to p_{*}\rho_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}
as in
[
CD16
, 4.4.2]
and we can test whether it is an isomorphism by taking the
\mathrm{Hom}(g_{\sharp}(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}),-)
for
g
weakly étale over
X
; the proof is then the same as in
[
CD16
, Proposition 4.4.3]
. As we already know that
h_{\sharp}
commutes with
\rho_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}
, we can reach the generators which finishes the proof.
∎

3.3 The rigidity theorem

Our goal is now to prove the rigidity theorem. As we have seen, both
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{(-)},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
and
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1},{\mathbb{G}_{m}}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{(}-),\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
are unstable qcqs motivic coefficient systems. Rigidity would imply Tate-stability and as a means of enforcing it, we further localize
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{(-)},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
at a certain set of maps. To see that this procedure still yields an unstable qcqs motivic coefficient system, we first need a bit of general theory.

Let
\mathcal{C}\colon\mathrm{Sch}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}})
be an unstable qcqs motivic coefficient system.
Fix a small collection of morphisms in
T
in
\mathcal{C}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}))
.
Then we may define an induced functor
\mathcal{C}_{T}\colon\mathrm{Sch}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}})
via

f\colon Y\to\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})\mapsto\mathcal{C}_{T}(Y)\coloneqq\mathcal{C}(Y)[\{f^{*}\lambda\otimes M\mid\lambda\in T;M\in\mathcal{C}(Y)\}^{-1}].

Remark 3.3.1
.

Note that there is a natural equivalence of functors

\mathcal{C}_{T}\simeq\mathcal{C}(-)\otimes_{\mathcal{C}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}))}\mathcal{C}_{T}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})).

Proposition 3.3.2
.

In the above setting, also
{\mathcal{C}}_{T}
is an unstable qcqs motivic coefficient system.

Proof.

This is immediate once one realises that all properties of being an unstable qcqs motivic coefficient system are properties internal to the
(\infty,2)
-category
\mathbf{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{\mathcal{C}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}))}
. As the functor
-\otimes_{\mathcal{C}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}))}\mathcal{C}_{T}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}))
is an (
\infty
,2)-functor (
[
HSS17
, Section 4.4]
), the claim follows.
∎

Recall that
\Pi\mathcal{P}
is the set of integers which are divisible only by primes in
\mathcal{P}
.

Definition 3.3.3
.

The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{eff}}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
of
effective solid motives
over
X
is the localization of the
\infty
-category

\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
at the maps

\Lambda[{\mathbb{G}_{m}}_{X}]\otimes_{\Lambda}\Lambda[Y]\simeq\Lambda[{\mathbb{G}_{m}}_{Y}]\to(\lim_{n\in\Pi\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}[{\mathbb{G}_{m}}_{X}])\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}}\Lambda[Y]

where
Y
is weakly smooth over
X
.

Remark 3.3.4
.

1.

Note that by
3.2.7
, the objects
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}[{\mathbb{G}_{m}}_{X}]
are actually in the image of the functor
\rho_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}
. Moreover, because
\rho_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}
commutes with limits by
3.2.3
, we see that
\lim_{n}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}[{\mathbb{G}_{m}}_{X}]
is again in the image of
\rho_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}
. Using that over the small pro-étale site,
pullbacks commute with all limits, we see that the maps we invert are stable under pullbacks.

2.

Note also that in fact, if
f
is a map that we invert, and
M
is any object, then the map
f\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{M}
is also inverted. In particular, the localization functor is symmetric monoidal.

Remark 3.3.5
.

Assume that all the primes in
\mathcal{P}
are invertible on
X
. By
[
CD16
, Proposition 3.2.2]
we have
\mathbb{Z}(1)[1]\simeq{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
in
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{eff}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}),\mathbb{Z})
. Thus for
n\in\Pi\mathcal{P}
, the classical Kummer sequences identifies
\mathbb{Z}(1)[1]/n\simeq\mu_{n}[1]
(see
[
CD16
, 3.2]
). In particular, we obtain maps
\mathbb{Z}(1)\to\mu_{n}
for all
n\in\Pi\mathcal{P}
. As both sides a stable under pullbacks, those maps exist over any scheme
X
. Their images in
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
provides after taking a limit a map

\Lambda(1)\to(\lim_{n\in\Pi\mathcal{P}}\mu_{n})\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}}\Lambda.

Thus, in
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
, the map

\Lambda[{\mathbb{G}_{m}}_{X}]\otimes_{\Lambda}\Lambda[Y]\to(\lim_{n\in\Pi\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}[{\mathbb{G}_{m}}_{X}])\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}}\Lambda[Y]

we invert can in fact be written as

\Lambda\oplus\Lambda(1)[1]\to(\lim_{n\in\Pi\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}\oplus\lim_{n\in\Pi\mathcal{P}}\mu_{n})\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}}\Lambda[Y].

We have
{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}}=\lim_{n\in\Pi\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
in
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}})^{\blacksquare}
because this is true over the small site, and
\rho_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}
commutes with limits.
Moreover,
\mu_{n}
comes from the small étale site, and the limit
\lim_{n\in\Pi\mathcal{P}}\mu_{n}
is representable in the small pro-étale site by
\mu_{\infty,\mathcal{P}}
. This proves that our localization is also the localization of
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
at the maps

M(1)\to M\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}}\mu_{\infty,\mathcal{P}}

for all
M
.

The discussion above in particular shows that the functor

\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{eff}}(-,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\colon\mathrm{Sch}\to\mathrm{CAlg}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}})

is as in
3.3.2
, for the map
\Lambda[{\mathbb{G}_{m}}_{\mathbb{Z}}]\to(\lim_{n\in\Pi\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}[{\mathbb{G}_{m}}_{\mathbb{Z}}])\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}}\Lambda
.

Proposition 3.3.6
.

The functor
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{eff}}(-,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
is a qcqs motivic coefficient system. Hence we have the six operations on
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{eff}}(-,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
in the sense of
2.5.4
.

Proof.

The only thing that does not follow from
3.3.2
is Tate-stability, but it is true since in
\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{eff}}(-,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
, the Tate twist is by definition equivalent to
\mu_{\infty,\mathcal{P}}\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}}\Lambda
which is invertible by
1.3.8
.
∎

Let us now state our first rigidity result:

Theorem 3.3.7
.

Assume that all prime numbers
\mathcal{P}
are invertible on
X
. Then, the functor
\rho_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\colon\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
induces an equivalence

\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{eff}}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}.

In particular, the
\mathrm{D}(-,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
are endowed with the six functors in the sense of
2.5.4
.

Proof.

We can assume that
\Lambda=\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}
.
To prove full faithfulness, we need to prove that for any
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
the canonical map

\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}}(\lim_{n\in\Pi\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}[{\mathbb{G}_{m}}_{X}],\rho_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\mathcal{F})\to\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}[\mathbb{G}_{m}],\rho_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\mathcal{F})

is an equivalence.
Since
\rho_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}
commutes with limits we may replace
\mathcal{F}
by
\tau_{\leq n}\mathcal{F}
.
We claim that both sides preserve filtered colimits in
\mathcal{F}
.
For the domain this follows immediately because the object
\lim_{n\in\Pi\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}[{\mathbb{G}_{m}}_{X}]
is dualizable in
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
, by
3.3.5
and
1.3.8
.
For the codomain, we observe that since the localization functor
\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})\to\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}
is symmetric monoidal, we have that

\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}[\mathbb{G}_{m}],\rho_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\mathcal{F})\simeq\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}[\mathbb{G}_{m}],\iota\rho_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\mathcal{F})

where we write
\iota\colon\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
for the inclusion.
To check compatibility with filtered colimits, it thus suffices to show that the functor

\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}[\mathbb{G}_{m}],\rho_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\tau_{\leq n}(-))\colon\mathrm{D}(X,\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})

commutes with filtered colimits.
This we may check after mapping out of
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}[W]
for a
w
-contractible weakly smooth scheme, since (shifts of) these compactly generate
\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})
.
Writing
p\colon W\to X
for the structure map and
\pi\colon W\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\to W
for the projection, we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}[W],\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}[\mathbb{G}_{m}],\rho_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\tau_{\leq n}(-)))

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{WSm}_{W},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}[\mathbb{G}_{m}],p^{*}\rho_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\tau_{\leq n}(-))

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{WSm}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}\times W},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}})^{\blacksquare}}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}},\pi^{*}p^{*}\rho_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}\tau_{\leq n}(-))

Now note that both sides are in the image of
\rho_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}
, since
\rho_{\sharp}^{\blacksquare}
commutes with pullback.
But then the claim follows from
[
HRS23
, Lemma 5.3]
.

Thus we may reduce to the case where
\mathcal{F}
is bounded and so, by dévissage, to the case where
\mathcal{F}
is concentrated in one degree.
Using
1.2.8
and commutation with filtered colimits again, we may reduce to the case where
\mathcal{F}
is torsion and the claim is then clear.
The proof of essential surjectivity is then exactly the same as in
3.2.7
.
∎

We finish by defining the category of solid motives and proving that it is the same as the category of effective solid motives.

Definition 3.3.8
.

We define the category of
solid motives

\mathrm{DM}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
to be the localization
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1},{\mathbb{G}_{m}}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
at the maps

M(1)\to M\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mathcal{P}}}\Sigma^{\infty}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\mu_{\infty,\mathcal{P}}

for all
M
.

Proposition 3.3.9
.

The canonical functor

\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{eff}}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\to\mathrm{DM}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

It suffices to show that
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1},{\mathbb{G}_{m}}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
is the
{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
-stabilization of
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
. Indeed, both categories will then have the same universal properties as presentably symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories because
\mu_{\infty,\mathcal{P}}
is
\otimes
-invertible already in the small pro-étale site.

The object
\Lambda(1)
is symmetric in
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
because it is the image of the object
\mathbb{Z}(1)
of
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{Sm}_{X},\mathbb{Z})
which is already symmetric by
[
Voe98
, Lemma 4.4]
. We can therefore compute the
{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
-stabilization as the colimit of the diagram:

\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\xrightarrow{-\otimes_{\Lambda}\Lambda(1)}\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\xrightarrow{-\otimes_{\Lambda}\Lambda(1)}\cdots.

On the other hand, we have a natural map from the colimit of the diagram

\underline{\mathrm{D}}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)\xrightarrow{-\otimes_{\Lambda}\Lambda(1)}\underline{\mathrm{D}}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)\xrightarrow{-\otimes_{\Lambda}\Lambda(1)}\cdots

to

\underline{\mathrm{DM}}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,\Lambda)
. This map is an equivalence because taking sections over any profinite set
S
is compatible with colimits (see the proof of
[
MW24
, Proposition 2.6.3.2]
applied to condensed
S
-categories) and over
S
, this is true by definition. As the condensed tensor product commutes with small colimits in each variable and as the global section functor commutes with colimits, this implies that
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1},{\mathbb{G}_{m}}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
indeed is the
{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
-stabilization of
\mathrm{D}^{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(\mathrm{WSm}_{X},\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}
finishing the proof.
∎

Theorem 3.3.10
.

The obtained realization functor

\rho_{\blacksquare}\colon\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{DM}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}\simeq\mathrm{D}(X,\Lambda)^{\blacksquare}

is a morphism of
6
-functor formalisms on qcqs
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}[1/\mathcal{P}])
-schemes. That is, it commutes with
f^{*}
,
\otimes
, and
g_{!}
for
g
a finitely presented map of qcqs schemes.

Proof.

By construction, the functor
\rho_{\blacksquare}
is a morphism of qcqs motivic coefficient systems.
In particular, it commutes with the functor
f^{*}
for each map
f
of schemes, with
\otimes
, and with
f_{\sharp}
if
f
is a smooth map of schemes (in fact, it is clear that it also commutes with
j_{\sharp}
for
j
a weakly étale map).
Using the localization triangle, we see that it also commutes with
i_{*}
for
i
a finitely presented closed immersion of qcqs schemes.
Using ambidexterity, one then concludes that it commutes with
p_{*}
for any finitely presented projective morphism of qcqs schemes.
Using the refined Chow lemma (
[
Aut18
,
Lemma 2020
]
), this implies that it commutes with
p_{*}
with
p
any proper map of finite presentation between qcqs schemes. Thus, the functor
\rho_{\blacksquare}
commutes with
f_{!}
for any finitely presented map of qcqs schemes. To obtain a morphism of
6
-functor formalisms, one may use the fact that
[
CLL25
, Theorem 4.35]
is an equivalence of categories, in particular it is functorial, so that
\rho_{\blacksquare}
lifts to a natural transformation between functors on correspondences.
∎

Proposition 3.3.11
.

Let
X
be a qcqs scheme over which
\ell
is invertible. Then the composition

\rho_{\blacksquare}\colon\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})^{\blacksquare}

lands in
the category
\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
and coincides with the
\ell
-adic realization functor defined in
[
CD16
]
.

Proof.

By
[
RT24a
, Corollary 3.7]
, for any object
M\in\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
, there exists a stratification on which
M
is dualizable. We claim that this implies that
\rho_{\blacksquare}(M)
is
\ell
-complete. Indeed, any dualizable object of
\mathrm{D}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})^{\blacksquare}
is
\ell
-complete because the unit is complete, and then we can use
1.1.6
together with the fact that pullback functors preserves limits to conclude. Thus, to show that
\rho_{\blacksquare}(M)
is an object of
\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
, it suffices to check that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, the object
\rho_{\blacksquare}(M)/\ell^{n}=\rho_{\blacksquare}(M/\ell^{n})
is a bounded complex of torsion constructible étale sheaves.
This means that we reduced the question to showing that the image of the functor

\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\xrightarrow{-/\ell^{n}}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}\mathbb{Z}}(\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}))\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}\mathbb{Z}}(\mathrm{D}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})^{\blacksquare})

lands in
\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}\mathbb{Z})
. But using rigidity for étale motives (
[
BH21
]
), the functor

\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}\mathbb{Z}}(\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z}))\to\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}\mathbb{Z}}(\mathrm{D}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})^{\blacksquare})

can be identified with the functor

\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}(X,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\blacksquare}.

By
1.3.9
, the above functor is fully faithful after left completion, in particular, it is fully faithful on bounded objects.
As the image of
M
is then a dualizable object of
\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}\mathbb{Z})
, we conclude that it is a bounded complex of constructible étale sheaves.

We now prove that
\rho_{\blacksquare}
coincides with the functor
\rho_{\ell}
of
[
CD16
]
. Recall that the latter is the restriction to constructible motives of the
\ell
-completion functor

\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z})^{\wedge}_{\ell}\simeq\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z})^{\wedge}_{\ell}.

Moreover, as

\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})\simeq\lim_{n}\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}\mathbb{Z})

by
[
HRS23
, Theorem 7.7]
, it suffices to prove that for each
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, the two functors
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{D}^{b}_{c}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}\mathbb{Z})
are equivalent in a way compatible with
n
. This was already proven in the previous paragraph: we have shown that for
M\in\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
, the object
\rho_{\blacksquare}(M)/\ell^{n}
is nothing but
M/\ell^{n}\in\mathrm{D}(X_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}\mathbb{Z})
.
∎

Corollary 3.3.12
.

We have a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Z})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})^{\blacksquare}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{Q})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{D}(X,\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})^{\blacksquare}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\blacksquare}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes\mathbb{Q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes\mathbb{Q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\blacksquare}}

of symmetric monoidal left adjoints which restricts to the classical square

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

where
\rho_{\ell}
is the
\ell
-adic realization functor of
[
CD16
]
and
\rho_{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}}
is the
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
-adic realization functor of
[
RS20
, 2.1.2]
.

Proof.

The first square exists formally. That it restricts to the second square can be seen as follows. We have already seen that the restriction of
\rho_{\blacksquare}
to
\mathrm{DM}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t},\mathrm{gm}}(X,\mathbb{Z})
is
\rho_{\ell}
. The claim about the vertical functors is formal. For the low horizontal functor, this follows from the definition (as the rationalization of
\rho_{\ell}
) and
[
HS23
, Corollary 2.4]
.
∎

Remark 3.3.13
.

Over schemes of characteristic zero, the above
3.3.12
also holds with
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}
and the ring of finite adeles
\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q},f}
instead of
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
and
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
. By using right adjoints and a bit of work, this allows for another definition of the algebra
\mathscr{N}\in\mathrm{DM}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{Q}),\mathbb{Z})
such that modules over it are Nori motives, as considered in
[
RT24b
]
. In forthcoming work, we plan to study a spectral analogue of the constructions of this paper, that would lead to a definition of spectral Nori motives.
